**Перевод:**

**That one Brahman shines as** ***māyā*** **with** ***sattva, rajas*** **and** ***tamas,*** **as** ***sūtra,*** **as** ***mahat-tattva,*** **as** ***ahaṅkāra,*** **and as powerful energies by taking the form of** ***devatās,*** **senses, sense objects and material qualities. That supreme Brahman is the cause of all the gross effects and all the subtle causes.**

**Комментарий:**

**Bhagavat Sandarbha 14:**

Brahman is the possessor of many intrinsic energies or antaraṅga-śaktis (*urū-śaktiḥ*). The word *eva* indicates that the śakti belongs to Brahman, and thus cannot be illusory. This means that the śakti is natural or inherent to the Lord. The cause is explained. He is superior to the external manifestation—the gross world of earth and other elements and he is superior to the subtle cause prakṛti. The manifestation from his svarūpa is Vaikuṇṭha etc. And the manifestation from taṭastha is the pure jīva. Without his śakti, the material world, spiritual world and jīvas would not exist or function.

What form do the śaktis take? They manifest as the mahat-tattva from jñāna-śakti (sattva), as sūtra from kriyā-śakti (rajas), as tan-mātras in the form of artha (objects--tamas) and all together as prakṛti. These are the gross and subtle forms. The Lord is superior in the form of two entities as the result (*phala*): the puruṣārtha, namely Bhagavān with his powers (conscious entity), and the pure jīva (conscious entity) because the jīva follows after the Lord. Thus his many śaktis manifest as jñāna, kriyā etc.

The śaktis’ intrinsic dependence on the Lord is made clear with evidence. In the beginning there was one Brahman (*ekam*). It took three forms: sattva, rajas and tamas. Sūtra arises from the kriyā-śakti (rajas) and mahat-tattva arises from jñāna-śakti (sattva). Ahaṅkāra (*aham*) arises from tamas. The Vedas speak of these and the jīva in its pure form, as well as Vaikuṇṭha (though not mentioned directly) as follows. *Sadaiva saumyedam agra āsīd*: the universe existed previously as the Lord. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad)

In the beginning (*adau)* there was one. Then the various forms arose. This shows the natural relationship of the śaktis to the Lord. Since nothing else existed the Lord could not be subject to any conditions. Though the Lord remains eternally perfect with his limbs by the power of his svarūpa, he is the material cause and the source of all matter, since everything in existence depends on his existence, just the like sun rays depend upon the existence of the sun. *Tasya bhāsā sarvam idaṁ vibhāti*: by his light all the world is illuminated. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad [2.2.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/katha-bs/2.2.15))

The inconceivable nature of his śaktis and their natural relation to the Lord is described in Viṣṇu Purāṇa. Maitreya asks:

*nirguṇasyāprameyasya śuddhasyāpy amalātmanaḥ \|*  
*kathaṁ sargādi-kartādi-kartṛtvaṁ brahmaṇo’bhyupagamyata \|\|*

**How can Brahman be the agent of creation and destruction of the universe since he is without guṇas, immeasurable, pure and without fault? (Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**1.3.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/1.3.1)**)**

Parāśara answers:

*śaktayaḥ sarva-bhāvānām acintya-jñāna-gocarāḥ \|*  
*yato’to brahmaṇas tās tu sargādyā bhāva-śaktayaḥ \|*  
*bhavanti tapasāṁ śreṣṭha pāvakasya yatoṣṇatā \|\|*

**O best of ascetics! The śaktis of things in this world are in the realm of inconceivability. From these arise the powers of Brahman to create the universe. It is like the power of heat in fire. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**1.3.2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/1.3.2)**)**

The commentary says:

It was said that Brahman created the universe. However the Lord is without guṇas, is unlimited by time or space (*aprameya*), is devoid of body (*śuddha*) or assistants, is devoid of impressions of sin or piety (*amalātmanaḥ*), and is devoid or rāga and dveṣa. How can this Brahman create the material universe? Such a person would not create a pot in the material world. The doubt is then dispelled. In this world, the śaktis of gems and mantras (*sarva-bhāvānām*) is in the realm of knowledge (*jñāna-gocaraḥ*) not subject to argument (acintya--inconceivable). The proof is simply that the effect does not take place without the gem. Or it is knowledge in the realm of inference from circumstance, since it is impossible to contemplate the śakti in terms difference or non-difference (*acintya)*. Such śaktis of Brahman which are accomplished by the Lord’s nature (*bhāva-śaktayaḥ*) are the cause of creation of the universe. Similarly fire has the power to burn.

Thus creation arises from Brahman who possesses inconceivable śakti and upon whom the guṇas are dependent. Śruti says:

*na tasya kāryaṁ karaṇaṁ ca vidyate*  
*na tat-samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate \|*  
*parāsya śaktir vividhaiva śrūyate*  
*svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca \|\|*

**He has no body and no senses. No one is equal to him or higher. His supreme power is manifold. His actions are naturally revealed with knowledge and strength. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad)**

*māyāṁ tu prakṛtiṁ vidyān māyinaṁ tu maheśvaram \|\|*

**Know prakṛti which is māyā and that supreme Lord who controls māyā. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad** [**4.10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/4.10)**)**

Or another meaning is as follows. There are śaktis in the realm of inconceivable knowledge for all substances, like the power to burn in fire. The śaktis of Brahman are non-different from his svarūpa (*bhāva-śaktayaḥ*). Śruti says *parāsya śaktir vividhāiva śrūyate*: it is said that the Lord has various śaktis. No one can refuse the power of gems and mantras just as one cannot refuse the example of fire and burning power. Thus the Lord has unlimited powers. Śruti says:

*sa vā ayam asya sarvasya vaśī sarvasyeśānaḥ sarvasyādhipatiḥ*

**He controls everything. He is the Lord of all, the controller of all. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad** [**4.4.22**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/4.4.22)**)**

Because of Brahman *(brahmaṇaḥ*), creation takes place (*sargādyā bhavanti*). There is no deficiency in this reasoning.

The question assumes that Brahman is without qualities. The answer assumes that Brahman has qualities. Thus the question is valid when it states Brahman is pure, without any qualities. In the answer, that is refuted and Brahman is established with śaktis (or qualities). In the question Brahman has no śaktis. The question has a different reference: Brahman is without material or spiritual qualities and is thus beyond proof. And thus it is pure (*śuddha*), without fault, not something which appears otherwise by influence of another object, like crystal next to a colorful object. The question takes support of Brahman without qualities.

The refutation first rejects Brahman without qualities and accepts śaktis of Brahman in order to accomplish Brahman’s agency. Then, in order to present qualities in Brahman an example is given. The qualities in Brahman are not like temporary heat in water but rather like the intrinsic heat of fire. Śruti says *na tat samaś cābhyadhikaś ca dṛśyate*: nothing equal or superior to the Lord is seen. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad)

There is some difference between Brahman and mantras or gems. The śaktis of Brahman are not under the control of anyone except the Lord (whereas mantras are invoked by various people). That is shown. Moreover, there is no fault at all in Brahman since creation is accomplished with transformations of the guṇas, just by the influence of the svarūpa-śakti.

The word Brahman means *sarvam khalv idam brahma*: Brahman is everything. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad) However, it is accepted that material guṇas are different from Brahman, and since he is said to be nirguṇa that means that he is untouched by the material guṇas. The guṇas belong to the external śakti.

There is the śruti statement *māyāṁ tu prakṛtiṁ vidyān mayinaṁ tu maheśvaram:* one should know māyā, prakṛti, and the Lord, the controller of māyā. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad) This indicates that māyā is under the Lord’s control.

*bhakti-yogena manasi samyak praṇihite ’male \|*  
*apaśyat puruṣaṁ pūrṇaṁ māyāṁ ca tad-apāśrayam \|\|*

**In that mind which was undisturbed because it was pure from the performance of bhakti, he saw Lord Kṛṣṇa, complete with all his energies and spiritual expansions, and also saw māyā who took shelter behind the Lord. (SB** [**1.7.4**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.4)**)**

Because the Lord is *maheśvara,* the supreme controller, māyā is under his shelter. It is controlled by him. Thus as previously concluded, it is proved that the Lord is not touched by the faults of māyā and has as his nature śaktis. Gītā says:

*jñeyaṁ yat tat pravakṣyāmi yaj jñātvāmṛtam aśnute \|*  
*anādi mat-paraṁ brahma na sat tan nāsad ucyate \|\|*

**I will now speak about the object of knowledge, knowing which you will attain liberation. The object of knowledge, the supreme Brahman, is beyond material cause and effect. (BG** [**13.13**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/13.13)**)**

Here is the arrangement. There is one supreme tattva which is present at all times by his natural, inconceivable śakti, in four forms—the Lord, his powers, the jīva and pradhāna. It is like the sun which has internal heat and produces rays externally as its secondary form.

*eka-deśa-sthitasyāgner jyotsnā vistāriṇī yathā \|*  
*parasya brahmaṇaḥ śaktis tathedam akhilaṁ jagad \|\|*

**Just as the light of a fire situated in one place spreads its illumination, the śakti of the supreme Brahman spreads out as the whole universe. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**1.22.55**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/1.22.55)**)**

Śruti says *yasya bhāsā sarvam idaṁ vibhāti*: by his light this whole universe shines. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad [2.2.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/katha-bs/2.2.15))

The argument that śaktis cannot coexist with the Lord since the Lord is all-pervading is refuted by the inconceivable nature in his śaktis. Inconceivable means “to perform the impossible.” There are three types of śakti: antaraṅga, bahiraṅga and taṭastha. These three give rise to four manifestations. By the antaraṅga-śakti, called svarūpa-śakti, the Lord’s complete svarūpa and as well, his powers such as Vaikuṇṭha (svarūpa-vaibhava) manifest. The taṭastha-śakti manifests as the individual pure jīvas, like rays of the sun. The bahiraṅga or māyā-śakti manifests as pradhāna with insentient, external objects like various colors in its rays. The jīva is taṭastha-śakti since he is similar to the Lord. Pradhāna is included in māyā. Viṣṇu Purāṇa describes these three:

*viṣṇu-śaktiḥ parā proktā kṣetra-jñākhyā tathāparā \|*  
*avidyā-karma-saṁjñāyā tṛtīyā śaktir iṣyate \|\|*

**The Lord has a superior energy, another energy called the jīva and a third energy, the material energy, called avidyā-karma. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa.** [**6.7.61**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/6.7.61)**)**

*tayā tirohitatvāc ca śaktiḥ kṣetra-jña-saṁjñitā \|*  
*sarva-bhūteṣu bhūpāla tāratamyena vartate*

**Concealed by māyā, the jīva-śakti exists in various conditions in various bodies. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**6.7.63**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/6.7.63)**)**

That which has ignorance as its effect is called māyā. Though this śakti is bahiraṅga, it is capable of covering the jīva, who belongs to the taṭastha-śakti. Thus the jīva exists in various bodies which act as coverings, from Brahmā to plants, in insignificant or important forms.

*yayā sammohito jīva ātmānaṁ tri-guṇātmakam \|*  
*paro ’pi manute ’narthaṁ tat-kṛtaṁ cābhipadyate \|\|*

**Bewildered by that māyā, the jīva, though separate from the three guṇas, considers himself made of the three guṇas and takes on material existence created by the guṇas. (SB** [**1.7.5**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.5)**)**

Bewildered by the inconceivable māyā *(yayā*) the jīva considers himself made of the guṇas. It will be shown later that pradhāna is made manifest through māyā.

Though the Lord’s svarūpa, his spiritual manifestation (*vaibhava*), the jīvas and matter, manifested from antaraṅga, taṭastha and bahiraṅga śaktis, are one with the Lord, and are thus non-different from the Lord, they are not equal in all respects. Thus they are localized portions of the Lord. Nor does the Lord take the form of these śaktis. Therefore their faults do not apply to the Lord.

### Text 38

*nātmā jajāna na mariṣyati naidhate 'sau*  
*na kṣīyate savana-vid vyabhicāriṇāṁ hi*  
*sarvatra śaśvad anapāyy upalabdhi-mātraṁ*  
*prāṇo yathendriya-balena vikalpitaṁ sat*

**Перевод:**

**The** ***jīva*** **does not undergo birth, death, growth, or deterioration since he is the seer of all conditions of changing bodies. That** ***jīva*** **is knowledge alone, existing constantly in the body, but endowed with activity by the power of the senses, just as** ***prāṇa*** **is one but moves from body to body.**

**Комментарий:**

**Tattva Sandarbha 53:**

The pure jīva (*ātmā*) is not born (*na jajāna*). Since he is not born, he has no changes that take place after birth. He does not increase (*naidhate*). Since he has no increase, there is no transformation since (*hi*) he is the seer (*savana-vit)* all times of various bodies with changes of infancy, youth etc. (*vyabhicārinām*). This means that the seer of these conditions does not become the condition. What is this ātmā without these conditions? He is a form of knowledge alone (*upalabdhi-mātram*) existing in a body (*sarvatra*) at all times *(śaśvat*).

“Knowledge of blue disappears and knowledge of yellow appears. With such perception, knowledge cannot be constant (*anapāyi*).” By the strength of the senses, one’s knowledge takes on variety. In this way the forms of blue and yellow, arising from functions of the senses, arise and disappear. That is not knowledge. The difference because of change (of body) and no change (jīva) is the first inference of the jīva and the Lord. The second inference is the difference between the seer and the seen. An example is given to show the unchanging state of the seer in changing situations (the seen). It is like prāṇa, which is one but moves to various bodies.

### Text 39

*aṇḍeṣu peśiṣu taruṣv aviniściteṣu*  
*prāṇo hi jīvam upadhāvati tatra tatra*  
*sanne yad indriya-gaṇe 'hami ca prasupte*  
*kūṭa-stha āśayam ṛte tad-anusmṛtir naḥ*

**Перевод:**

***Prāṇa*** **follows the** ***jīva*** **in bodies born from eggs, wombs, seeds and perspiration. When the senses and** ***ahaṅkāra*** **cease to function in deep sleep, the fixed** ***ātmā*** **alone remains, without the covering of the subtle body, but with memory of the experience of deep sleep.**

**Комментарий:**

The memory or witness *(smṛtiḥ*) of deep sleep of the indivisible ātmā (*tat*) arises for us (*naḥ),* the seers in the waking state. We think, “For that time I slept and was not aware of anything.” There is this awareness of ātmā in deep sleep because of not remembering any particular experience. It is not clear awareness since there is lack of relationship with objects. Like the manifestation of self-revealing objects like the sun, this awareness of the ātmā, being simply awareness of the self, takes shelter of itself.

*yad vai tan na paśyati paśyan vai draṣṭavyān na paśyati, na hi draṣṭur dṛṣṭor viparilopo vidyate*

**He certainly does not see. Although seeing, he does not see what is to be seen. The seeing capacity of the seer is not lost. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad** [**4.3.23**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/4.3.23)**)**

The third inference for ātmā is the difference between the witness and what is witnessed (seen in the above example). The fourth inference for ātmā can be understood to be the difference between the sufferer (because of attachment to the body) and the shelter of prema (experienced apart from the body).

There are four inferences in terms of positive and negative conclusions (ātmā is puruṣārtha, body is not puruṣārtha). The first inference for ātmā is by seeing the difference between the temporary and permanent. The second inference is through the difference between the seer and the seen. The third inference is through the difference between the witness and the witnessed. The fourth inference which gives awareness of happiness is through the difference between the suffering jīva and the jīva as the shelter of prema.

### Text 40

*yarhy abja-nābha-caraṇaiṣaṇayoru-bhaktyā*  
*ceto-malāni vidhamed guṇa-karma-jāni*  
*tasmin viśuddha upalabhyata ātma-tattvaṁ*  
*śākṣād yathāmala-dṛśoḥ savitṛ-prakāśaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**After having had material desires at the lotus feet of the Lord, when one destroys the contaminations in the heart arising from** ***guṇa*** **and** ***karma*** **by pure** ***bhakti,*** **one directly realizes in the pure heart the form of Bhagavān, just like one sees the sun with purified eyes.**

**Комментарий:**

If one realized the formless in deep sleep, why does saṁsāra again appear? Is there an impression of ignorance remaining? Let that be. For kevala-jñānīs, with the destruction of ahaṅkāra by jñāna, there is a destruction of mahat-tattva and pradhāna.

Will there be no disturbance of the āśraya because no other effort exists, as in deep sleep? Since there is realization of the jīva, will Bhagavān also be realized?

When one gives up the four puruṣārthas, and cleanses the contaminations of the heart by bhakti, with a desire to attain his lotus feet, Bhagavān, the shelter of the ātmā (*ātma-tattva*) is realized by that influence in the heart, what to speak of realizing ātmā.

### Text 41

*śrī-rājovāca*  
*karma-yogaṁ vadata naḥ puruṣo yena saṁskṛtaḥ*  
*vidhūyehāśu karmāṇi naiṣkarmyaṁ vindate param*

**Перевод:**

**King Nimi said: You have said that one destroys contamination arising from** ***guṇa*** **and** ***karma.*** **I have understood that one becomes free of** ***karma*** **by** ***bhakti*** **and by** ***jñāna*****. I now ask how one can be purified of** ***karma*** **by** ***karma-yoga.***

**Комментарий:**

Prabuddha mentioned that kāma-karmas should be given up since the results are criticized. But nitya and naimittika karmas should not be given up. One should make them favorable to the highest goal in the mind.

### Text 42

*evaṁ praśnam ṛṣīn pūrvam apṛcchaṁ pitur antike*  
*nābruvan brahmaṇaḥ putrās tatra kāraṇam ucyatām*

**Перевод:**

**Once in the past, in the presence of my father, Mahārāja Ikṣvāku, I placed a similar question before four great sages who were sons of Lord Brahmā. But they did not answer my question. Please explain the reason for this.**

**Комментарий:**

Another doubt is raised. Because he was young, he was not qualified to receive the answer.

### Text 43

*śrī-āvirhotra uvāca*  
*karmākarma vikarmeti veda-vādo na laukikaḥ*  
*vedasya ceśvarātmatvāt tatra muhyanti sūrayaḥ*

**Перевод:**

***Karma, akarma*** **and** ***vikarma*** **are explained by the Vedas, but not by material people. Because the Vedas arise from the Lord, even the learned are bewildered about** ***karma.***

**Комментарий:**

He explains why the sons of Brahmā did not answer. *Vikarma* is not performing what should be done as explained in the Vedas (verse 46). Because the Vedas are the same as the Lord, being a sound representation of the Lord, people are bewildered.

*śabda-brahma para-brahma mamobhe śāśvatī tanū*

**The scripture and impersonal Brahman are my two eternal forms. (SB** [**6.16.51**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/16.51)**)**

*vedo nārāyaṇaḥ sākṣāt svayambhūr iti śuśruma*

**The Vedas are directly Nārāyaṇa and are born from him. This we have heard from Yamarāja. (SB** [**6.1.40**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/1.40)**)**

*asya mahato bhūtasya niśvasitam etad yad ṛg-vedaḥ*

**The Rg Veda is the breathing of the Lord. (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad)**

*tasmād yajñāt sarva-hutaḥ ṛcaḥ sāmāni jajñire chandāṁsi jajñire tasmād*

**The verses all arose from the Lord. The meters arose from him. (ṚG Veda)**

The Vedas arose from the Lord. Thus they are one with the Lord. Like an avatāra they are without human origin. They are devoid of the four faults starting with pramāda. Thus they are most inconceivable like the Lord. Thus even the most learned are bewildered.

### Text 44

*parokṣa-vādo vedo 'yaṁ bālānām anuśāsanam*  
*karma-mokṣāya karmāṇi vidhatte hy agadaṁ yathā*

**Перевод:**

**The Vedas, speaking indirectly, prescribe** ***karma-yoga*****, in order to liberate people from** ***karma,*** **just as a father promises candy in order to get children to drink medicine.**

**Комментарий:**

Having given up even nitya and naimittika karmas gradually, one should perform worship of the Lord. Karma-kāṇḍa is like instructing children who are not too intelligent and unsettled in mind. The Vedas prescribe karmas for destroying the beginningless chain of karma. The Vedas, non-different from the Lord, speak indirectly, since telling them not to perform nitya and naimittka karmas and do something else, and showing them that the results are secondary, will instill fear in people.

Purifying them by giving instructions of what to do and what not to do, insignificant results are given and not fear, in order to produce freedom from karmas full of suffering. This is done out of great affection. An example is given. The father gives medicine to the children who are not of his nature. He gives them candy in order that the drink the medicine to cure the disease. The goal is not the candy but drinking the medicine.

### Text 45

*nācared yas tu vedoktaṁ svayam ajño 'jitendriyaḥ*  
*vikarmaṇā hy adharmeṇa mṛtyor mṛtyum upaiti saḥ*

**Перевод:**

**If an ignorant person who has not conquered the material senses does not adhere to the Vedic injunctions, certainly he will engage in sinful and irreligious activities and attain repeated birth and death.**

**Комментарий:**

As the treatment of children is beneficial for the children and not following is not beneficial for them, not following the Vedas is not beneficial for the ignorant.

The person who is attached to sense enjoyment, who has not controlled his senses in this world, who is like a child, and who is thus without knowledge—who does not have faith in the hearing about the Lord, if he does not follow the Vedic rules, and rather follows adharma by not following nitya and naimittika acts, attains death without end. He gains no puṇya. It is said:

*tāvat karmāṇi kurvīta na nirvidyeta yāvatā*  
*mat-kathā-śravaṇādau vā śraddhā yāvan na jāyate*

**As long as one does not become detached from daily and periodic duties or has not awakened his faith in hearing topics about me, one must perform one’s prescribed duties of** ***varṇāśrama.*** **(SB** [**11.20.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/20.9)**)**

### Text 46

*vedoktam eva kurvāṇo niḥsaṅgo 'rpitam īśvare*  
*naiṣkarmyaṁ labhate siddhiṁ rocanārthā phala-śrutiḥ*

**Перевод:**

**A person who performs the** ***karmas*** **mentioned in the Vedas becoming detached from the results, offers the results to the Lord and attains destruction of all k*****armas.*** **Only for attracting the people are material results mentioned in the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

“Since karmas are all of the same type, will not karma increase by performing Vedic karmas? How does it free one from karma?” Both questions are answered. Attachment to the actions being done is rejected (*niḥsaṅgaḥ*). The material results are rejected. Thus karma does not increase and it leads to freedom from karma. This is accomplished by offering the actions to the Lord.

*āmayo yaś ca bhūtānāṁ jāyate yena suvrata \|*  
*tad eva hy āmayaṁ dravyaṁ na punāti cikitsitam \|\|*

**The substance by which a living being’s disease arises and which does not cure the disease, when combined with other substances in a medicine, destroys the disease. (SB** [**1.5.33**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.33)**)**

### Text 47

*ya āśu hṛdaya-granthiṁ nirjihīṛṣuḥ parātmanaḥ*  
*vidhinopacared devaṁ tantroktena ca keśavam*

**Перевод:**

**One who desires to cut the knot of false ego, which binds the spirit soul, should worship the Supreme Lord, Keśava, by the regulations found in literatures such as the Pañcarātra and the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

Having stated what is prescribed for the ignorant, hear what is advised for the intelligent. One should worship the deity of the Lord. The word *ca* means that one worships using pañcarātric (*tantrokten*a) and Vedic mantras.

### Text 48

*labdhvānugraha ācāryāt tena sandarśitāgamaḥ*  
*mahā-puruṣam abhyarcen mūrtyābhimatayātmanaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Having obtained the mercy of his** ***guru,*** **who reveals to the disciple the injunctions of Vedic scriptures, the devotee should worship the Supreme Lord in the particular personal form of the Lord the devotee finds most attractive.**

**Комментарий:**

In the general worship of the Lord one should use one’s preferred form.

### Text 50-51

*arcādau hṛdaye cāpi yathā-labdhopacārakaiḥ*  
*dravya-kṣity-ātma-liṇgāni niṣpādya prokṣya cāsanam*  
*pādyādīn upakalpyātha sannidhāpya samāhitaḥ*  
*hṛd-ādibhiḥ kṛta-nyāso mūla-mantreṇa cārcayet*

**Перевод:**

**The devotee should prepare properly obtained ingredients, the earth, the mind and the deity. He should sprinkle his sitting place with water for purification and prepare the foot water,** ***arghya, ācamana*** **and** ***madhuparka.*** **The devotee should then place the deity in the proper place, concentrate his attention, and then perform** ***nyāsa*** **using** ***mantras.*** **Then he should offer worship with the** ***mūla- mantra.***

**Комментарий:**

Placing the deity in a proper place (*sannidhāpya*), or within the mind thinking of the deity as Kṛṣṇa (*sannidhāpya*), one should perform nyāsa.

### Text 52-53

*sāṅgopāṅgāṁ sa-pārṣadāṁ tāṁ tāṁ mūrtiṁ sva-mantrataḥ*  
*pādyārghyācamanīyādyaiḥ snāna-vāso-vibhūṣaṇaiḥ*  
*gandha-mālyākṣata-sragbhir dhūpa-dīpopahārakaiḥ*  
*sāṅgam sampūjya vidhivat stavaiḥ stutvā named dharim*

**Перевод:**

**One should worship the deity along with each of the limbs of his body, his weapons such as the Sudarśana cakra, and his associates. One should worship each of these aspects of the Lord by its own** ***mantra*** **and with offerings of water to wash the feet,** ***arghya,*** **water to wash the mouth, water for bathing, fine clothing and ornaments, fragrant oils, jewel necklaces, unbroken garlands, incense and lamps. Having thus completed the worship in all its aspects in accordance with the prescribed regulations, one should then honor the deity with prayers and offer obeisances to him by bowing down.**

**Комментарий:**

Two verses describe the concluding actions of worship.

\|\| [11.3.54](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/3.54) \|\|

*ātmānam tan-mayam dhyāyan mūrtiṁ sampūjayed dhareḥ*  
*śeṣām ādhāya śirasā sva-dhāmny udvāsya sat-kṛtam*

**The worshiper should identify himself with the Lord and worship that form. Then he should take the remnants of the deity's paraphernalia, such as flower garlands, upon his head and respectfully establishing the deity back in his abode, he should complete the worship.**

One should identify with the Lord in the manner that the lusty person sees the world in terms of beautiful women. Placing the deity in his place, establishing using the śayana-mudrā etc., he should complete the worship. Or placing the deity in a special place and meditating on the sleeping pastimes, he should conclude the worship.

### Text 55

*evam agny-arka-toyādāv atithau hṛdaye ca yaḥ*  
*yajatīśvaram ātmānam acirān mucyate hi saḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Thus the worshiper of the Supreme Lord should recognize that the Lord is all-pervading and should worship him through his presence in fire, the sun, water and other elements, in the heart of the guest one receives in one's home, and also in one's own heart. In this way the worshiper will very soon achieve liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

Other possible elements of worship are described.

## Chapter Four

### Text 1

*śrī-rājovāca*  
*yāni yānīha karmāṇi yair yaiḥ svacchanda-janmabhiḥ*  
*cakre karoti kartā vā haris tāni bruvantu naḥ*

**Перевод:**

**King Nimi said: You should tell us about the activities which the Lord has performed in the past, is performing now and will perform in the future by his various births, chosen by his free will, in this world.**

**Комментарий:**

Previously it was said:

*śṛṇvan su-bhadrāṇi rathāṅga-pāṇer*  
*janmāni karmāṇi ca yāni loke*  
*gītāni nāmāni tad-arthakāni*  
*gāyan vilajjo vicared asaṅgaḥ*

**Hearing the most auspicious birth and activities of Kṛṣṇa which are sung by all people and singing his names which describe those activities while having no attachment to anything else, a person should without shame wander in the world. (SB** [**11.2.39**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/2.39)**)**

*labdhvānugraha ācāryāt tena sandarśitāgamaḥ*  
*mahā-puruṣam abhyarcen mūrtyābhimatayātmanaḥ*

**Having obtained the mercy of his** ***guru,*** **who reveals to the disciple the injunctions of Vedic scriptures, the devotee should worship the Supreme Lord in the particular personal form of the Lord the devotee finds most attractive. (SB** [**11.3.48**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/3.48)**)**

*gandha-mālyākṣata-sragbhir dhūpa-dīpopahārakaiḥ*  
*sāṅgam sampūjya vidhivat stavaiḥ stutvā named dharim*

**Having thus completed the worship in all its aspects in accordance with the prescribed regulations, one should then honor the deity with prayers and offer obeisances to him by bowing down. SB** [**11.3.53**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/3.52)**)**

Now his activities are described so that glorification can take place.

### Text 3

*bhūtair yadā pañcabhir ātma-sṛṣṭaiḥ*  
*puraṁ virājaṁ viracayya tasmin*  
*svāṁśena viṣṭaḥ puruṣābhidhānam*  
*avāpa nārāyaṇa ādi-devaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**When the primeval Nārāyaṇa accepted the form of the** ***puruṣa,*** **he created the universe from the five elements produced from himself and then entered within the universe by his own portion.**

**Комментарий:**

The purusāvatara is described in general.

*nārāyaṇe turīyākhye bhagavac-chabda-śabdite*  
*mano mayy ādadhad yogī mad-dharmā vaśitām iyāt*

**The** ***yogī*** **who concentrates his mind on my form of Nārāyaṇa, known as the fourth factor, Bhagavān, obtains the mystic perfection called** ***vaśitā—*****not being influenced by the** ***guṇas,*** **since he possesses my qualities. (SB** [**11.15.16**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/15.16)**)**

When Bhagavān accepted the form of the puruṣa as the great creator, making the universe from the elements, he then entered as his aṁśa and accepted the form of a puruṣa.

### Text 4

*yat-kāya eṣa bhuvana-traya-sanniveśo*  
*yasyendriyais tanu-bhṛtām ubhayendriyāṇi*  
*jñānaṁ svataḥ śvasanato balam oja īhā*  
*sattvādibhiḥ sthiti-layodbhava ādi-kartā*

**Перевод:**

**Within the body of the puruṣa who has entered the universe reside universe composed of three planetary systems. By the Lord’s senses, the action and knowledge senses of the** ***jīvas*** **arise. From his expansion** ***antaryāmī,*** **the knowledge of the** ***jīvas*** **arises. From his** ***prāṇa*** **the physical and sensual strength and action of the j*****īvas*** **arise. He is the final cause of creation, maintenance and destruction through the three** ***guṇas.***

**Комментарий:**

The second puruṣa is described. *Yat* means the puruṣa who entered the universe. *Viṣṇos tu trīṇi rūpāni*: Visnu has three forms. (Nārada-tantra)

### Text 5

*ādāv abhūc chata-dhṛtī rajasāsya sarge*  
*viṣṇuḥ sthitau kratu-patir dvija-dharma-setuḥ*  
*rudro ’pyayāya tamasā puruṣaḥ sa ādya*  
*ity udbhava-sthiti-layāḥ satataṁ prajāsu*

**Перевод:**

**In the beginning, the** ***puruṣa*** **appeared as Brahmā through** ***rajoguṇa*** **for creation, as Viṣṇu, the lord of sacrifice, and protector of** ***dharma*** **of the** ***brāhmaṇas*****, for maintenance, and as Śiva through** ***tamo-guṇa*** **for destruction. In this way creation, maintenance and destruction of the living entities takes place at all times.**

**Комментарий:**

The guṇa avatāra is described. *Sarge* here means *visarge*, the creation within the universe. *Sattvena* is not mentioned though rajasā and tamasā are mentioned because the Lord has a pure form, and supervises sattva-guṇa by proximity only.

### Text 6

*dharmasya dakṣa-duhitary ajaniṣṭa mūrtyāṁ*  
*nārāyaṇo nara ṛṣi-pravaraḥ praśāntaḥ*  
*naiṣkarmya-lakṣaṇam uvāca cacāra karma*  
*yo ’dyāpi cāsta ṛṣi-varya-niṣevitāṅghriḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Nara-Nārāyaṇa, the best of sages, fully in control of their senses, were born to Dharma in the womb of Mūrti, the daughter of Dakṣa, They taught and performed actions with complete detachment and, worshipped by the best of sages, remain alive even today.**

**Комментарий:**

Līlā avatāras are now described. *Naiṣkarmya-laksaṇam karma* means actions of worshipping the Lord.

*tṛtīyam ṛṣi-sargaṁ vai devarṣitvam upetya saḥ \|*  
*tantraṁ sātvatam ācaṣṭa naiṣkarmyaṁ karmaṇāṁ yataḥ \|\|*

**The Lord, making his appearance among the sages as Nārada, produced the Pañcarātra scriptures from which one learns how devotional activities free one from the bondage of** ***karma.*** **(SB** [**1.3.8**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/3.8)**)**

### Text 11

*kṣut-tṛṭ-tri-kāla-guṇa-māruta-jaihva-śaiṣṇān*  
*asmān apāra-jaladhīn atitīrya kecit*  
*krodhasya yānti viphalasya vaśaṁ pade gor*  
*majjanti duścara-tapaś ca vṛthotsṛjanti*

**Перевод:**

**Some men practice severe penances to cross beyond our influence, which is like an immeasurable ocean with hunger, thirst, heat, cold rain brought about by the passing of time, sensuous wind and the urges of the tongue and sex organs. Nevertheless such persons fall under the control of useless anger and drown in the water of a cow’s hoof print. Thus they destroy the benefit of their difficult austerities and can neither enjoy nor attain liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

Anger is compared to the water in a cow’s footprint, because it is limited to injuring its object. Its objects are not unrestricted like the desire to taste etc.

### Text 21

*niḥkṣatriyām akṛta gāṁ ca triḥ-sapta-kṛtvo*  
*rāmas tu haihaya-kulāpyaya-bhārgavāgniḥ*  
*so ’bdhiṁ babandha daśa-vaktram ahan sa-laṅkaṁ*  
*sītā-patir jayati loka-mala-ghna-kīṛtiḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Paraśurāma appeared in the family of Bhṛgu as a fire that burned to ashes the dynasty of Haihaya and rid the earth of all** ***kṣatriyas*** **twenty-one times. The same Lord appeared as Rāmacandra, the husband of Sītādevī, subdued the ocean and killed the ten-headed Rāvaṇa, along with all the soldiers of Laṅkā. May that Rāma, whose glories destroy the contamination of the world, be always victorious!**

**Комментарий:**

He killed persons situated in Laṅka (*sa-laṅkam*). *Jayati* indicates eternal existence as in *jayati jana-nivasaḥ* (SB [10.90.48](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/90.48)).

### Text 22

*bhūmer bharāvataraṇāya yaduṣv ajanmā*  
*jātaḥ kariṣyati surair api duṣkarāṇi*  
*vādair vimohayati yajña-kṛto ’tad-arhān*  
*śūdrān kalau kṣiti-bhujo nyahaniṣyad ante*

**Перевод:**

**To diminish the burden of the earth, the unborn Lord will be born in the Yadu dynasty and perform feats impossible even for the** ***devatās.*** **Propounding speculative philosophy, the Lord, as Buddha, will bewilder the unworthy performers of Vedic sacrifices. And as Kalki, the Lord will kill all the low-class men posing as rulers at the end of the age of Kali.**

**Комментарий:**

Buddha will bewilder unqualified persons, who are śūdras and Kalki will kill śūdras posing as rulers*. Śūdra* means ferocious or mlecchas.

## Chapter Five

### Text 2

*śrī-camasa uvāca*  
*mukha-bāhūru-pādebhyaḥ puruṣasyāśramaiḥ saha*  
*catvāro jajñire varṇa guṇair viprādayaḥ pṛthak*

**Перевод:**

**Camasa said: Each of the four social orders, headed by the** ***brāhmaṇas,*** **was born through different combinations of the modes of nature, from the face, arms, thighs and feet of the Supreme Lord in his universal form, along with the** ***āśramas.***

**Комментарий:**

This is a description of the universal form. This is considered non-different from the antaryāmī. The different limbs are generally stated since it is said:

*gṛhāśramo jaghanato brahmacaryaṁ hṛdo mama*  
*vakṣaḥ-sthalād vane-vāsaḥ sannyāsaḥ śirasi sthitaḥ*

**The married order of life appeared from the loins of my universal form, and the celibate students came from my heart. The forest-dwelling retired order of life appeared from my chest, and the renounced order of life was situated within the head. (SB** [**11.17.14**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/17.14)**)**

### Text 3

*ya eṣāṁ puruṣaṁ sākṣād ātma-prabhavam īśvaram*  
*na bhajanty avajānanti sthānād bhraṣṭāḥ patanty adhaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**If any of the members of the four** ***varṇas*** **and four** ***āśramas*** **fail to worship the Lord and thus disrespect the Lord, who is the source of their own creation, they will fall down from their** ***āśrama.***

**Комментарий:**

Those who do not worship, in other words, who disrespect the Lord, fall down. Or some, not hearing about the Lord do not worship, and some, even though hearing, do not worship. They disrespect the Lord. They fall from their āśrama, and gradually go downwards.

### Text 4

*dūre hari-kathāḥ kecid dūre cācyuta-kīrtanāḥ*  
*striyaḥ śūdrādayaś caiva te ’nukampyā bhavādṛśām*

**Перевод:**

**Persons devoid of devotee association and who cannot appreciate glorification of the Lord, such as women and** ***śūdras,*** **deserve your mercy.**

**Комментарий:**

He shows mercy to some.

### Text 5

*vipro rājanya-vaiśyau vā hareḥ prāptāḥ padāntikam*  
*śrautena janmanāthāpi muhyanty āmnāya-vādinaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**On the other hand,** ***brāhmaṇas, kṣatriyas*** **and** ***vaiśyas,*** **even after being allowed to study the Vedas by receiving Vedic initiation, become bewildered since they interpret the meaning of the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

He is indifferent to others. The followers of Jaimini and karma-kāṇda who are atheists, not understand the meaning brahma-kāṇḍa, become bewildered.

### Text 6

*karmaṇy akovidāḥ stabdhā mūrkhāḥ paṇḍita-māninaḥ*  
*vadanti cāṭukān mūḍhā yayā mādhvyā girotsukāḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Those ignorant of how to act without accruing results since they are proud and foolish, and think themselves learned, speak sweet words among themselves because of being bewildered by the promising words of the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

The bewilderment is described.

### Text 7

*rajasā ghora-saṅkalpāḥ kāmukā ahi-manyavaḥ*  
*dāmbhikā māninaḥ papa vihasanty acyuta-priyān*

**Перевод:**

**Due to the influence of the mode of passion, they become subject to terrible vows and are excessively lusty. Their anger is like that of a snake. Deceitful, overly proud, and sinful in their behavior, they mock the devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

The gradual fall down is described in three verses. This verse gives general criticism. The next verse criticizes the brāhmaṇas. The third verse criticizes the kṣatriyas and vaiśyas.

### Text 10

*sarveṣu śaśvat tanu-bhṛtsv avasthitaṁ*  
*yathā kham ātmānam abhīṣṭam īśvaram*  
*vedopagītaṁ ca na śṛṇvate ’budhā*  
*mano-rathānāṁ pravadanti vārtayā*

**Перевод:**

**These unintelligent people do not hear about the Lord, worthy of worship and proclaimed in the Vedas, who is situated in all beings at all times, but who is not affected, like the sky. Instead they speak continuously about material topics.**

**Комментарий:**

Their neglect of the Lord arising from their bewilderment is described. He situated as the Lord in all beings at all times. This is realized by the wise.

### Text 11

*loke vyavāyāmiṣa-madya-sevā*  
*nityā hi jantor na hi tatra codanā*  
*vyavasthitis teṣu vivāha-yajña*  
*surā-grahair āsu nivṛttir iṣṭā*

**Перевод:**

**In this world, people are always attracted to sex, meat eating and intoxication. This is not a rule in the scriptures, but there is an arrangement through marriage and sacrificial drinking so that they can eventually give up these acts.**

**Комментарий:**

There is no rule concerning these items, but it is possible to perform these acts, as if there were rules. Marriage is given as an example of vidhi. And in marriage one approaches the wife at the proper time (*niyama*). If there is a niyama clarifying the vidhi, one should follow it. If scripture orders to use a horse harness (*parisaṅkhyā*) to clarify a niyama one must follow the injunction.

The rule is given because of the attachment to eating meat, though the ultimate goal is to give up meat eating completely. *Vyavasthiti* in the verse indicates a rule (niyama), where there is no alternative. *Parisaṅkhya* indicates the choice where there are alternatives within a niyama. A niyama is established (to make some restriction) because people have constant attraction for enjoyment. If attached persons act without considering vidhi (necessary to follow), they act without authorization.

*vidhir atyantam aprāptau niyamaḥ pākṣike sati \|*  
*tatra cānyatra samprāptau parisaṅkhyā vidhīyate \|\|*

**A** ***vidhi*** **is a rule that cannot be established except by the scriptural statement itself (*****apūrva-vidhi*****). A** ***vidhi*** **that favors one alternative over others is called** ***niyama.*** **A** ***vidhi*** **is called** ***parisankhyā*** **when it specifies one among different alternatives. (Tantra-vārtika, Kumārila)**

An example is of the first type is “One should perform *sandhyā* rites daily.” That which is obligatory in all cases is called apūrva-vidhi. An example of niyama is the following “One should take the rope of sacrificial the animal.” The brāhmaṇa could choose a horse or donkey but there is the niyama to take the halter of a horse. An example of parisaṅkhya is “One should eat five types of animals with five nails.” This rule is for persons attached to eating meat. It is restricted to five animals. This gives permission (if one is inclined to eat meat), but is not an order to eat meat.

*aprāpta-vidhir evāyam ato mantraś ca niścitaḥ \|*  
*parisaṅkhyā phalenoktā na viśeṣaḥ punaḥ śruteḥ \|\|*

**When an injunction is determined in meaning to be non-obligatory, it is called parisaṅkhya as a result. It should not have a special condition, since that would make the rule meaningless. (Tantra-vārtika)**

“Generally two ropes become a halter.” This is a special condition. One cannot say that by parisaṅkhya others rules are negated. Personally one can give up eating five nailed animals. But that does not mean that everyone must give up eating the animals.

Persons attached to enjoyment become detached by following the niyama. Through the niyama the parisaṅkhya develops its meaning.

The meaning of the verse is this. Humans have constant attachment of sex etc. Sex is natural, whereas meat eating and intoxication are practiced by influence of others. There is no apūrva-vidhi or compulsory injunction concerning these (*codanā*). But there are arrangements for this by marriage etc. Through niyamas, parisaṅkhyas are enacted. One has marriage and as well, in marriage, sex at the proper time. Sacrifice involve drinking liquor, as at the sautrāmani sacrifice.

“If there is a parisaṅkhya it became a rule for sex life etc. Why should these acts be given up and criticized?” The goal is to give up these acts (*āsu*). Permission is given because of the attachment. Not like apūrva-vidhis and niyama, the parisaṅkhya is not obligatory simply because it is given in scripture. The goal is to give up the actions.

It is said that it is a fault to reject the wife during her period. The fault applies if one simply neglects the rule. The fault does not occur if the rule is neglected because of detachment. It is also a fault to neglect the rule because of lack of inclination for relations with one’s wife and attraction to another woman. It is like the sin of killing a brāhmaṇa. Eating meat and drinking liquor should be considered in the same way.

\|\| [11.5.19](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.19) \|\|

*śrī rājovāca*  
*kasmin kāle sa bhagavān kiṁ varṇaḥ kīdṛśo nṛbhiḥ*  
*nāmnā vā kena vidhinā pūjyate tad ihocyatām*

**King Nimi said: In what colors and forms does the Supreme Lord appear in each of the different ages, with what names and by what types of rules is the Lord worshiped by men?**

By what rules is he worshipped by ordinary men? At all times the Lord is worshipped by extraordinary persons and even other avatāras.

\|\| [11.5.27](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.27) \|\|

*dvāpare bhagavāñ śyāmaḥ pīta-vāsā nijāyudhaḥ*  
*śrīvatsādibhir aṅkaiś ca lakṣaṇair upalakṣitaḥ*

**In Dvāpara-yuga the Supreme Lord, with a dark blue complexion, wearing yellow garments, is marked with Śrīvatsa and other distinctive ornaments and bodily features.**

Speaking of the Dvāpara yuga avatāra, he indicates the special yuga with the special appearance of Kṛṣṇa. In most cases the Dvāpara avatāra is green like a parrot feather and the Kali-yuga avatāra is śyāma.

*dvāpare śuka-patrābhaḥ kalau śyāmaḥ prakīrtitaḥ*

**In Dvāpara the avatāra is the color of a parrot feather, and in Kali the color is śyāma. (Viṣṇu Dharmottara)**

\|\| [11.5.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.28) \|\|

*taṁ tadā puruṣaṁ martyā mahā-rājopalakṣaṇam*  
*yajanti veda-tantrābhyāṁ paraṁ jijñāsavo nṛpa*

**O King! In Dvāpara-yuga men who desire to know the Supreme Lord worship him in the mood of honoring a great king, following the prescriptions of both the Vedas and Pañcarātra scriptures.**

They worship the supreme Lord (*paraṁ puruṣam*) as a great king. Hari-vaṁśa describes his installation as a king. Those who desire to realize him (the goal of the worship) worship in this way.

\|\| [11.5.29-30](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.29) \|\|

*namas te vāsudevāya namaḥ saṅkarṣaṇāya ca*  
*pradyumnāyāniruddhāya tubhyaṁ bhagavate namaḥ*  
*nārāyaṇāya ṛṣaye puruṣāya mahātmane*  
*viśveśvarāya viśvāya sarva-bhūtātmane namaḥ*

**Obeisances to you, Vāsudeva, to your forms of Saṅkarṣaṇa, Pradyumna and Aniruddha, to Nārāyaṇa Ṛṣi, to the** ***puruṣa,*** **best of personalities, the master of this universe, the form of the universe, the soul within all created entities.**

His nature as Kṛṣṇa is made clear through the form of the catur-vyūha. He is described as the source of various avatāras. Nārāyaṇa refers to Nārāyaṇa Ṛṣi. Having given a general idea, he is described as the source of the puruṣāvatāras, manifesting unlimited avatāras (*puruṣāya mahātmane*). Thus he is the lord of all the universes and the form of all the universes (*viśvāya*). What more can be said? He is the ātmā of all beings (*sarva-bhūta-rūpāya*).

\|\| [11.5.31](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.31) \|\|

*iti dvāpara urv-īśa stuvanti jagad-īśvaram*  
*nānā-tantra-vidhānena kalāv api tathā śṛṇu*

**O King! In this way people in Dvāpara-yuga glorified the Lord of the universe. Now kindly hear about the form in Kali-yuga, worshipped through methods of worship explained in various Pañcarātra scriptures.**

The praise is done by sages in knowledge from the beginning of Dvāpara, in which he appears.

\|\| [11.5.32](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.32) \|\|

*kṛṣṇa-varṇaṁ tviṣākṛṣṇaṁ sāṅgopāṅgāstra-pārṣadam*  
*yajñaiḥ saṅkīrtana-prāyair yajanti hi su-medhasaḥ*

**The intelligent people worship the Lord, black in color, but shining with brilliance, who is accompanied by his associates, weapons, major and minor limbs, chiefly through chanting.**

The Kali-yuga avatāra appearing after Kṛṣṇa is described.

**Tattva Sandarbha 1:**

The intelligent people of Kali-yuga worship this person who is golden (*akṛṣṇam)* by his complexion (*tviṣā*). His golden complexion is described elsewhere:

*āsan varṇās trayo hy asya gṛhṇato ’nuyugaṁ tanūḥ*  
*śuklo raktas tathā pīta idānīṁ kṛṣṇatāṁ gataḥ*

**Your son Kṛṣṇa appeared in previous yugas with three different colors—white, red and yellow—and now he has appeared in a blackish color. (SB** [**10.8.13**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/8.13)**)**

Thus the verse in the Eleventh Canto confirms what is stated in the Tenth Canto, since the Tenth Canto verse speaks of the avatāra appearing in Dvāpara-yuga as Kṛṣṇa, and the Eleventh Canto verse states that Śukla appeared in Satya-yuga and Rakta appeared in Tretā-yuga. The yellow avatāra mentioned by Garga is the form of Caitanya who appeared in a previous day of Brahmā (since the past tense is used).

The Eleventh Canto describes Kṛṣṇa, with śyāma complexion, with qualities of a great king, and having the catur-vyūha, as the form to be worshipped in Dvāpara-yuga:

*dvāpare bhagavāñ śyāmaḥ pīta-vāsā nijāyudhaḥ*  
*śrīvatsādibhir aṅkaiś ca lakṣaṇair upalakṣitaḥ*

**In Dvāpara-yuga the Supreme Lord, with a dark blue complexion, wearing yellow garments, is marked with Śrīvatsa and other distinctive ornaments and bodily features.**

*taṁ tadā puruṣaṁ martyā mahā-rājopalakṣaṇam*  
*yajanti veda-tantrābhyāṁ paraṁ jijñāsavo nṛpa*

**O King! In Dvāpara-yuga men who desire to know the Supreme Lord worship him in the mood of honoring a great king, following the prescriptions of both the Vedas and Pañcarātra scriptures.**

*namas te vāsudevāya namaḥ saṅkarṣaṇāya ca*  
*pradyumnāyāniruddhāya tubhyaṁ bhagavate namaḥ*

**Obeisances to you, Vāsudeva, and to your forms of Saṅkarṣaṇa, Pradyumna and Aniruddha. (SB** [**11.5.27-29**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.27)**)**

In Viṣṇu-dharma it is said that the Lord appears in green color in Dvāpara-yuga and dark blue in Kali-yuga. It should be understood that this description applies to Dvāpara-yugas when Kṛṣṇa himself does not incarnate (as the yugāvatāra). When Kṛṣṇa appears, Gaurāṅga appears in the next Kali-yuga. This is the explanation. This means that, without doubt, Gaurāṅga is a special manifestation of Svayam Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa himself.

This is what is described in Viṣṇu-dharmottara:

*pratyakṣa-rūpa-dhṛg-devo dṛśyate na kalau hariḥ \|*  
*kṛtādiṣv eva tenaiva triyugaḥ paripaṭhyate \|\|*  
*kaler ante ca samprāpte kalkinaṁ brahma-vādinām \|*  
*anupraviśya kurute vāsudevo jagat-sthitim \|\|*

**The Lord is not directly visible in Kali-yuga. Thus the Lord is described as appearing in the three other yugas. At the end of Kali-yuga, Vāsudeva enters into Kalki among the brahmavādīs and makes his residence in the world.**

Caitanya supersedes the Kali-yuga form by having the unlimited powers of Kṛṣṇa, since it is shown that he inevitably appears at the beginning of Kali-yuga after Kṛṣṇa’s appearance.

The nature of his appearance is made explicit by the description. He is in the category of Kṛṣṇa (*kṛṣṇa-varṇam*) or can be addressed by the two syllables (*varṇa*) “Kṛṣṇa” to indicate that he is none other than Kṛṣṇa. *Varṇa* is used to indicate syllables, as in the following:

*samāhutā bhīṣmaka-kanyayā ye*  
*śriyaḥ savarṇena bubhūṣayaiṣām*

**The kings had assembled, attracted by the beauty of Rukmiṇī, who can be equated with the syllables of the word Lakṣmī, with a desire to take her as their bride. (SB** [**3.3.3**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/3.3)**)**

Śrīdhara Svāmī says, “The two syllables (*rukmi*) in Rukmiṇī’s name express the same meaning as Śrī.”

Or *kṛṣṇa-varṇam* can mean that Caitanya describes (*varṇayati)* Kṛṣṇa. He sings about him under the influence of joy on remembering Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes of great bliss and teaches glorification of Kṛṣṇa to all people by his great mercy. Or he teaches about Kṛṣṇa by his excellent beauty of golden color. This means that, by seeing Caitanya of golden complexion, everyone has realization of Kṛṣṇa.

Or though he is golden in complexion to everyone’s vision, he is blackish, Śyāmasundara, to special devotees by a particular manifestation (*tviṣā)*. In other words, Caitanya is a direct avatāra of Kṛṣṇa himself because of manifesting the complete form of Kṛṣṇa within himself.

His nature as Bhagavān is made clear with the rest of the description. He is seen at all times with innumerable great devotees (*sāṅga*) from Gauḍa, Varendra, Suhma (parts of Bengal) and Utkala (Orissa). They are identified as his limbs *(aṅga*) because they are most attractive (like the Lord himself). They are identified as his ornaments (*upaṅgas)* because they have great powers (like gems) and they are identified as his weapons because they are always with him. These are his associates (*pārṣadam*). Or he always exists with his associates (*pārṣadam*) like Advaita who are like his limbs, his ornaments or his weapons, since they are the source of the greatest prema.

How do they worship this form of the Lord? They worship using the ingredients of worship (*yajñaiḥ*).

*na yatra vaikuṇṭha-kathā-sudhāpagā*  
*na sādhavo bhāgavatās tadāśrayāḥ*  
*na yatra yajñeśa-makhā mahotsavāḥ*  
*sureśa-loko 'pi na vai sa sevyatām*

**Even Brahmaloka should not be accepted as a residence if there are no rivers of sweet topics about the Lord, no devotees who take shelter of those topics, and no great festivals or sacrifices. (SB** [**5.19.23**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/19.23)**)**

The ingredients of worship are described. The worship is predominated by singing about Kṛṣṇa, experiencing joy by that singing, in a huge group of people. The abhidheya is clearly indicated by showing that the devotees constantly worship the Lord mainly by saṅkīrtana.

In the thousand names of Viṣṇu this avatāra is indicated in the names.

*suvarṇa-varṇo hemāṅgo varāṅgaś candanāṅgadī \|*  
*sannyāsa-kṛt samaḥ śānto niṣṭhā-śānti-parāyaṇaḥ \|\|*

**He had a form like gold (*****suvarṇa-varṇaḥ*****). He had places desirable as gold (*****hemāṅgaḥ*****). He had beautiful limbs (*****varāṅgaḥ*****). He had armlets which please the devotees’ hearts (*****candanāṅgad*****ī). He tooks sannyāsa (*****sannyasa-kṛt*****). He reflected on the confidential pastimes of the Lord (*****śamaḥ*****). He was detached from everything except Kṛṣṇa (*****śāntaḥ*****). He was fixed in the sacrifice of chanting the Lord’s names (*****niṣṭhā*****). He pacified the advaitins who were against bhakti by performing kīrtana (*****śāntaḥ*****). He was the highest refuge for person of various bhāvas, up to mahābhāva (*****parāyaṇam*****).**

The most learned Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya said:

*kālān naṣṭa bhakti-yogaṁ nijaṁ yaḥ*  
*prāduṣkartuṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanya-nāmā \|*  
*āvirbhūtas tasya pādāravinde*  
*gāḍhaṁ gāḍhaṁ līyatāṁ citta-bhṛṅgaḥ \|\|*

**Let my consciousness, which is like a honeybee, take shelter of the lotus feet of the Lord who has just now appeared as Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu to teach devotional service to himself. This system had almost been lost due to the influence of time.**

### Text 34

*tyaktvā su-dustyaja-surepsita-rājya-lakṣmīṁ*  
*dharmiṣṭha ārya-vacasā yad agād araṇyam*  
*māyā-mṛgaṁ dayitayepsitam anvadhāvad*  
*vande mahā-puruṣa te caraṇāravindam*

**Перевод:**

**O great** ***puruṣa*****! I offer respects to the lotus of feet of you who gave up Sītā who was worshipped by the** ***devatās*** **and hard to give up. I offer respects to you who, fixed in** ***dharma,*** **went to the forest, obeying the words of your father, and who pursued a false deer desired by Sitā.**

**Комментарий:**

O Mahāprabhu! I offer respects to the lotus feet of you who gave up a wife hard to give up, whose devotion to you was desired by the *devatās*. I offer respects to you who, fixed in *dharma*, obeyed the curse of a *brāhmaṇa,* went to the forest and pursued materialistic men to give them *prema* by your mercy.

Your mercy and protection of proper conduct is not a new thing

### Text 36

*kaliṁ sabhājayanty āryā guṇa jñāḥ sāra-bhāginaḥ*  
*yatra saṅkīrtanenaiva sarva-svārtho ’bhilabhyate*

**Перевод:**

**Those who are actually advanced in knowledge, who know quality of this age, who have accepted the best, show the quality of Kali-yuga because in this fallen age all perfection of life can easily be achieved by the performance of** ***saṅkīrtana.***

**Комментарий:**

Those who know the quality of chanting the name, who accept only the essence and not the faults, show (*sabhājayanti*) the quality of Kali-yuga, in which all goals which are achieved by a thousand sādhanas such as meditation in other yugas, are attained by kīrtana.

### Text 37

*na hy ataḥ paramo lābho dehināṁ bhrāmyatām iha*  
*yato vindeta paramāṁ śāntiṁ naśyati saṁsṛtiḥ*

**Перевод:**

**For the conditioned souls wandering in this world, there is no greater attainment than** ***saṅkīrtana*****, from which one obtains** ***bhakti,*** **full of peace, and by which** ***saṁsāra*** **is destroyed.**

**Комментарий:**

Kīrtana is glorified. There is no higher gain for people wandering about than kīrtana (*ataḥ*). From kīrtana (*yataḥ*) one can become fixed in the Lord (*paramām śāntim*), the highest goal, which is not attained by meditation etc., and as a secondary result saṁsāra is destroyed. *Śamo man-niṣṭhatā-buddheḥ:* śama means being fixed in the Lord. (SB [11.19.36](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/19.36))

### Text 38-40

*kṛtādiṣu prajā rājan kalāv icchanti sambhavam*  
*kalau khalu bhaviṣyanti nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇāḥ*  
*kvacit kvacin mahā-rāja draviḍeṣu ca bhūriśaḥ*  
*tāmraparṇī nadī yatra kṛtamālā payasvinī*  
*kāverī ca mahā-puṇyā pratīcī ca mahā-nadī*  
*ye pibanti jalaṁ tāsāṁ manujā manujeśvara*  
*prāyo bhaktā bhagavati vāsudeve ’malāśayāḥ*

**Перевод:**

**O King! The inhabitants of Satya-yuga and other ages eagerly desire to take birth in this age of Kali, since in this age there will be many devotees of the Supreme Lord, Nārāyaṇa. These devotees will appear in various places but will be especially numerous in South India and places like Bengal. O master of men, in the age of Kali those persons who drink the waters of the holy rivers of South India, such as the Tāmraparṇī, Krtamālā, Payasvinī, the extremely pious Kāverī and the Pratīcī Mahānadī, will almost all be pure hearted devotees of the Supreme Lord Vāsudeva.**

**Комментарий:**

**Bhakti Sandarbha 273:**

Thus hearing that the people of Kali-yuga could attain excellent steadiness in Bhagavān, the people living during those yugas pray for birth in Kali-yuga to attain that steadiness. *Nārāyaṇa*-*parāyaṇāḥ* means devotees with intense prema for the Lord. That is revealed by the effect *paramāṁ śāntim* (mind fixed on the Lord SB [11.5.37](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.37)). That is also indicated in the following:

*muktānām api siddhānāṁ nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇaḥ*  
*su-durlabhaḥ praśāntātmā koṭiṣv api mahā-mune*

**O great sage! Greater than those who are jīvanmukta and greater than those who attain liberation is the devotee of Lord Nārāyaṇa. Such a devotee, who is fixed on the Lord, is rare even among ten million people. (SB** [**6.14.5**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/14.5)**)**

However, one should not say that kīrtana becomes glorious because of association with Kali-yuga since there is no restriction of time and place for bhakti.

*cakrāyudhasya nāmāni sadā sarvatra kīrtayet*

**One should chant the name of the Lord at all times and all places. (Viṣṇu-dharma)**

*na deśa-kālāvasthātma-śuddhy-ādikam apekṣate \|*  
*kintu svatantram evaidaṁ tan nāma kāmita-kāmadam \|\|*

**The name of the Lord, independent and fulfilling all desires, does not depend on time, place, condition or purity of body or mind. (Skanda Purāṇa)**

*kalau kṛta-yugaṁ tasya kalis tasya kṛte yuge \|*  
*yasya cetasi govindo hṛdaye yasya nācyuta \|\|*

**For one who has Govinda in his heart, Kali-yuga becomes Satya-yuga. For one who does not have the Lord in his heart, Satya-yuga becomes Kali-yuga. (Viṣṇu-dharma)**

One should not think that even a little effort in kīrtana gives great result simply because people cannot do any other sādhana in Kali-yuga. The great result arises because of the power of kīrtana itself.

*yasmin nyasta-matir na yāti narakaṁ svargo ’pi yac-cintane*  
*vighno yatra niveśitātma-manasāṁ brāhmo ’pi loko ’lpakaḥ \|*  
*muktiṁ cetasi yaḥ sthito’mala-dhiyāṁ puṁsāṁ dadāty avyayaḥ*  
*kiṁ citraṁ yad aghaṁ prayāti vilayaṁ tatrācyute kīrtite \|\|*

**Thinking of him, one does not go to hell. Thinking of him, one can go to Svarga. Concentrating on the Lord situated in the heart, the imperishable Lord gives liberation to people of impure mind. Is it astonishing that, by singing the name of the Lord, sin is destroyed? (Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**6.8.57**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/6.8.57)**)**

The Viṣṇu Purāṇa shows that kīrtana is greater than remembering the Lord even at its stage of perfection, samādhi.

*etan nirvidyamānānām icchatām akuto-bhayam \|*  
*yogināṁ nṛpa nirṇītaṁ harer nāmānukīrtanam \|\|*

**O King! Continuous chanting of the name of the Lord has been prescribed for devotees, for those with desires for liberation and material elevation, and for those satisfied with the self. (SB** [**2.1.11**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/1.11)**)**

It is also said:

*aghacchit-smaraṇaṁ viṣṇor bahv-āyāsena sādhyate \|*  
*oṣṭha-spandana-mātreṇa kīrtanaṁ tu tato varam \|\|*

**Remembering Viṣṇu to destroy sin is accomplished with great endeavor. Kīrtana, by vibrating the lips, is better than that. (Vaiṣṇava-cintāmaṇi)**

Elsewhere it is said:

*yena janma-śataiḥ pūrvaṁ vāsudevaḥ samarcitaḥ \|*  
*tan-mukhe hari-nāmāni sadā tiṣṭhanti bhārata \|\|*

**O Bhārata! The person who has worshipped Vāsudeva for a hundred lives will have the name of the Lord on his lips.**

*sarvāparādha-kṛd api mucyate hari-saṁśrayāt \|*  
*harer apy aparādhān yaḥ kuryād dvipada-pāṁsanaḥ \|\|*  
*nāmāśrayaḥ kadācit syāt taraty eva sa nāmataḥ \|*  
*nāmno ’pi sarva-suhṛdo hy aparādhāt pataty adhaḥ \|\|*

**A person who commits all offenses is freed by taking shelter of the Lord. If a person, personified evil, commits offenses to the Lord, by taking shelter of the name, he is delivered by the name. By offending the name even a person who is friendly to all beings falls to hell.**

Thus in all yugas, kīrtana has the same potency. In Kali-yuga, by the mercy of the Lord, this method is to be accepted. Thus it is praised for Kali-yuga.

Though other processes of bhakti are to be performed, they are done along with kīrtana:

*kṛṣṇa-varṇaṁ tviṣākṛṣṇaṁ sāṅgopāṅgāstra-pārṣadam*  
*yajñaiḥ saṅkīrtana-prāyair yajanti hi su-medhasaḥ*

**The intelligent people worship the Lord, black in color, but shining with brilliance, who is accompanied by his associates, weapons, major and minor limbs, by various types of worship but chiefly through chanting. (SB** [**11.5.32**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.32)**)**

Nāma-kīrtana is especially recommended, since it is independent:

*harer nāma harer nāma harer nāmaiva kevalam \|*  
*kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva nāsty eva gatir anyathā \|\|*

**The name of the Lord, the name of the Lord, the name of the Lord alone is the shelter. There is no other shelter in Kali-yuga. (Nāradīya Purāṇa)**

Therefore it was rightly said:

*kaliṁ sabhājayanty āryā guṇa jñāḥ sāra-bhāginaḥ*  
*yatra saṅkīrtanenaiva sarva-svārtho ’bhilabhyate*

**Those who are actually advanced in knowledge, who know quality, who have accepted the best, praise Kali-yuga because in this fallen age all perfection of life can easily be achieved by the performance of saṅkīrtana. (SB** [**11.5.36**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.36)**)**

*na hy ataḥ paramo lābho dehināṁ bhrāmyatām iha*  
*yato vindeta paramāṁ śāntiṁ naśyati saṁsṛtiḥ*

**For the conditioned souls wandering in this world, there is no greater attainment than saṅkīrtana, from which one obtains bhakti, full of peace, and by which saṁsāra is destroyed. (SB** [**11.5.37**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.37)**)**

These devotees do not commit without offense.

### Text 41

*devarṣi-bhūtāpta-nṛṇāṁ pitṝṇāṁ*  
*na kiṅkaro nāyam ṛṇī ca rājan*  
*sarvātmanā yaḥ śaraṇaṁ śaraṇyaṁ*  
*gato mukundaṁ parihṛtya kartam*

**Перевод:**

**O King! One who has given up all** ***varṇāśrama*** **duties and has taken full shelter of the lotus feet of Mukunda, who offers shelter to all, is not a debtor to or servant of the** ***devatā*****s, great sages, ordinary living beings, relatives or Pitṛs.**

**Комментарий:**

He who is surrendered is not the servant of the devatās, sages, living beings, relatives or Pitṛs. He is a servant of the Lord only. He is not qualified to do anything else. He is independent of devatās and others. It is said:

*ayaṁ devo munir vandya eṣa brahmā bṛhaspatiḥ \|*  
*ity ākhyā jāyate tāvad yāvan nārcayate harim \|\|*

**As long as one does not worship the Lord one should worship the devatās, sages, Brahmā and Bṛhaspati. (Garuḍa Purāṇa)**

### Text 42

*sva-pāda-mūlam bhajataḥ priyasya*  
*tyaktānya-bhāvasya hariḥ pareśaḥ*  
*vikarma yac cotpatitaṁ kathañcid*  
*dhunoti sarvaṁ hṛdi sanniviṣṭaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**One who has thus given up all other engagements and has taken full shelter at the lotus feet of Supreme Lord is very dear to the Lord. If such a surrendered soul accidentally commits some sinful activity, the Supreme Lord, great because of his affection for the devotee, seated within everyone’s heart, immediately takes away the reaction to such sin.**

**Комментарий:**

It is not necessary to do any other action, such as atonement for vikarma since by surrender to the Lord one lacks any tendency to commit vikarma. If accidently a person commits a vikarma, by remembering the Lord, atonement is achieved as an accompanying result, since the Lord resides in the heart. Because the person has given up other actions and has devotion to the Lord, the vikarma is destroyed. The Lord by nature destroys all faults (*hariḥ*) and possesses all śakti (*pareśaḥ*). The devotee is the object of his affection (*priyasya*).

### Text 45

*tvam apy etān mahā-bhāga dharmān bhāgavatān śrutān*  
*āsthitaḥ śraddhayā yukto niḥsaṅgo yāsyase param*

**Перевод:**

**O greatly fortunate Vasudeva! Simply apply with faith these principles of devotional service which you have heard, and thus, being free from material association, you will attain the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

According to the question, he has spoken bhagavad-dharma which benefits others and then answers for Vasudeva particularly. You however (*api*) will attain the supreme Lord. The real meaning is “You will not attain the Lord, since you have him eternally.”

### Text 46

*yuvayoḥ khalu dampatyor yaśasā pūritaṁ jagat*  
*putratām agamad yad vāṁ bhagavān īśvaro hariḥ*

**Перевод:**

**The whole world has become filled with the glories of you and your good wife because the Supreme Lord has taken the position of your son.**

**Комментарий:**

Rather you have made the universe successful with your glories, because the Lord became your son during the prakaṭa pastimes.

### Text 47

*darśanāliṅganālāpaiḥ śayanāsana-bhojanaiḥ*  
*ātmā vāṁ pāvitaḥ kṛṣṇe putra-snehaṁ prakurvatoḥ*

**Перевод:**

**You and your wife Devakī, having manifested love for Kṛṣṇa and accepted him as your son, have purified your selves by seeing him, embracing him, speaking with him, resting with him, sitting together with him and taking your meals with him**

**Комментарий:**

“But I have fear of the offense to brāhmaṇas, as in the case of Jaya and Vijaya.”

You have been purified of apparent fault (*pāvitaḥ*). Actually you are protected, far from fault since you have affection for Kṛṣṇa as your son. You are greatly different from others.

### Text 48

*vaireṇa yaṁ nṛpatayaḥ śiśupāla-pauṇḍra-*  
*śālvādayo gati-vilāsa-vilokanādyaiḥ*  
*dhyāyanta ākṛta-dhiyaḥ śayanāsanādau*  
*tat-sāmyam āpur anurakta-dhiyāṁ punaḥ kim*

**Перевод:**

**Inimical kings like Śiśupāla, Pauṇḍraka and Śālva, while they were lying down, sitting or engaging in other activities, enviously meditated upon the bodily movements of the Lord, his sporting pastimes, and his loving glances. Being thus always absorbed in Kṛṣṇa, they achieved positions in the spiritual world. What then can be said of the benedictions offered to those who constantly fix their minds on Lord Kṛṣṇa in a favorable, loving mood?**

**Комментарий:**

Let us not speak more of you two. Since Kṛṣṇa is the most attractive form of the Lord, even ordinary people attain a destination like his, what to speak of the Yadus. With kaimutya, he shows the destination of the lamentable demons.

### Text 49

*māpatya-buddhim akṛthāḥ kṛṣṇe sarvātmanīśvare*  
*māyā-manuṣya-bhāvena gūḍhaiśvarye pare ’vyaye*

**Перевод:**

**Do not think that the Supreme Lord, the soul of all beings, who conceals his powers, makes a show of appearing to be your son. Having human form as his** ***svarūpa,*** **he hides is powers but remains with undiminished powers.**

**Комментарий:**

The reason for everything is given. Do not think of him as an ordinary son since Kṛṣṇa, source of all existence and bliss (*kṛṣir bhū-vācakaḥ*) is the source of all avatāras (*sarvātmani*), and thus the controller of everyone (*īśvare)*, and supreme. He has power impossible to know (*gūḍhaiśvarye*) by manifesting human pastimes by his mercy (*māyā-manuṣya-bhāvena*). Thus he is inexhaustible (*avyaye*).

Or “The two had affection for the Lord as their son. But is that affection as a son to be praised?” Do not think otherwise. Think of him as a son, since he is Kṛṣṇa, full of all power and bliss, and performs pastimes as a human by his mercy.

### Text 50

*bhū-bhārāsura-rājanya- hantave guptaye satām*  
*avatīrṇasya nirvṛtyai yaśo loke vitanyate*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord descended to kill the demoniac kings who were the burden of the earth, to protect the saintly devotees, and to award liberation to the demons and** ***prema*** **to the devotees. His fame has spread throughout the universe.**

**Комментарий:**

His predominance of mercy is described.

### Text 51

*śrī-śuka uvāca*  
*etac chrutvā mahā-bhāgo vasudevo ’ti-vismitaḥ*  
*devakī ca mahā-bhāgā jahatur moham ātmanaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Having heard this narration, the greatly fortunate Vasudeva was completely struck with wonder. Thus he and his most blessed wife Devakī gave up their ignorance concerning the curse.**

**Комментарий:**

They gave up their ignorance (*moham*) concerning fear from a brāhmaṇa’s curse.

### Text 52

*itihāsam imaṁ puṇyaṁ dhārayed yaḥ samāhitaḥ*  
*sa vidhūyeha śamalaṁ brahma-bhūyāya kalpate*

**Перевод:**

**Anyone who meditates on this pure historical narration with attention will destroy ignorance in this very life and thus achieve liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

One achieves that which is the greatest (*brahma-bhūyāya*). One becomes the greatest devotee.

## Chapter Six

### Text 1

*śrī-śuka uvāca*  
*atha brahmātma-jaiḥ devaiḥ prajeśair āvṛto ‘bhyagāt*  
*bhavaś ca bhūta-bhavyeśo yayau bhūta-gaṇair vṛtaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Śukadeva Gosvāmī said: Lord Brahmā then set off for Dvārakā, accompanied by his sons as well as by the** ***devatās*** **and the great Prajāpatis. Śiva, the bestower of auspiciousness to all living beings, also went, surrounded by many ghostly creatures.**

**Комментарий:**

The topic begins in order to describe in detail knowledge of the Lord (*atha*).

### Text 2-4

*indro marudbhir Bhagavān ādityā vasavo ’śvinau*  
*ṛbhavo ’ṅgiraso rudrā viśve sādhyāś ca devatāḥ*  
*gandharvāpsaraso nāgāḥ siddha-cāraṇa-guhyakāḥ*  
*ṛṣayaḥ pitaraś caiva sa-vidyādhara-kinnarāḥ*  
*dvārakām upasañjagmuḥ sarve kṛṣṇa-didṛkṣavaḥ*  
*vapuṣā yena bhagavān nara-loka-manoramaḥ*  
*yaśo vitene lokeṣu sarva-loka-malāpaham*

**Перевод:**

**The powerful Indra, along with the Maruts, Ādityas, Vasus, Aśvinīs, Ṛbhus, Aṅgirās, Rudras, Viśvedevas, Sādhyas, Gandharvas, Apsarās, Nāgas, Siddhas, Cāraṇas, Guhyakas, the great sages and forefathers and the Vidyādharas and Kinnaras, arrived at the city of Dvārakā, hoping to see Lord Kṛṣṇa. By his form, Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord, enchanted all human beings, spread his own fame throughout the worlds, and destroyed all contamination within the universe.**

**Комментарий:**

The verses describe the arrival at Dvārakā. By his form (*yena vapusā*) Kṛṣṇa, including in himself the avatāras, spread his fame.

### Text 5

*tasyāṁ vibhrājamānāyāṁ samṛddhāyāṁ maharddhibhiḥ*  
*vyacakṣatāvitṛptākṣāḥ kṛṣṇam adbhuta-darśanam*

**Перевод:**

**In that resplendent city of Dvārakā, rich with all superior wealth, the** ***devatās*** **beheld with unsatiated eyes the wonderful form of Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

They saw Kṛṣṇa who was astonishing to see.

*yan martya-līlaupayikaṁ sva-yoga-*  
*māyā-balaṁ darśayatā gṛhītam*  
*vismāpanaṁ svasya ca saubhagarddheḥ*  
*paraṁ padaṁ bhūṣaṇa-bhūṣaṇāṅgam*

**Kṛṣṇa possesses that form suitable for human pastimes to show the full capacity of his** ***yoga-māyā.*** **That form astonishes even the lord of Vaikuṇṭha. It is the pinnacle of auspicious qualities and enhances the beauty of his ornaments. (SB** [**3.2.12**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.12)**)**

### Text 8

*tvaṁ māyayā tri-guṇayātmani durvibhāvyaṁ*  
*vyaktaṁ sṛjasy avasi lumpasi tad-guṇa-sthaḥ*  
*naitair bhavān ajita karmabhir ajyate vai*  
*yat sve sukhe ’vyavahite ’bhirato ’navadyaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**O unconquerable Lord! You create, maintain and destroy the inconceivable universe by** ***māyā*** **made of the three** ***guṇa*****s, all within your own self. You are situated in the** ***guṇas of*** ***māyā*** **but are not contaminated by your actions since you, who cannot be criticized, are engaged in unobstructed bliss.**

**Комментарий:**

You are engaged in your unobstructed bliss, revealed by hlādini-śakti arising from your svarūpa (*sve sukhe*).

### Text 9

*śuddhir nṛṇāṁ na tu tatheḍya durāśayānāṁ*  
*vidyā-śrutādhyayana-dāna-tapaḥ-kriyābhiḥ*  
*sattvātmanām ṛṣabha te yaśasi pravṛddha-*  
*sac-chraddhayā śravaṇa-sambhṛtayā yathā syāt*

**Перевод:**

**O worshipable Lord! Those whose consciousness is polluted by pride cannot purify themselves by knowledge, study of the Vedas, charity, austerity or ritual activities. O best among the** ***avatāra*** **with pure bodies*****!*** **Pure souls who have developed a deep, excellent faith in your glories, nourished by hearing scripture, become completely purified.**

**Комментарий:**

Those who are contaminated, lacking faith in pure bhakti, cannot be purified by knowledge etc. whereas persons without knowledge, if they have bhakti, are purified, even just by hearing, what to speak of seeing the Lord. You show your pastimes to give happiness to the devotees.

### Text 10

*syān nas tavāṅghrir aśubhāśaya-dhūmaketuḥ*  
*kṣemāya yo munibhir ārdra-hṛdohyamānaḥ*  
*yaḥ sātvataiḥ sama-vibhūtaya ātmavadbhir*  
*vyūhe ’rcitaḥ savanaśaḥ svar-atikramāya*

**Перевод:**

**May the lotus feet of your lordship whom sages contemplate with hearts melted by** ***prema*** **to attain the highest bliss*****,*** **and whom devotees, accepting you as their master, worship in four forms three times a day for attaining** ***prema*** **and for giving up desire for Svarga, burn up our desire for enjoyment!**

**Комментарий:**

You are worshipped by ātmārāmas for attaining the highest happiness (*kṣemāya*) and worshipped by the devotees (*sātvataiḥ*) who accept you as their master (*ātmavadbhiḥ*) for surpassing Svarga and attaining the form of the highest happiness, the wealth of the devotees (*sama-vibhūtaye*). *Sama* means devotee as in SB [6.17.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-6/17.28):

*nārāyaṇa-parāḥ sarve na kutaścana bibhyati*  
*svargāpavarga-narakeṣv api tulyārtha-darśinaḥ*

**Devotees solely engaged in the service of Nārāyaṇa never fear any condition of life. For them the heavenly planets, liberation and the hellish planets are all the same.**

### Text 11

*yas cintyate prayata-pāṇibhir adhvarāgnau*  
*trayyā nirukta-vidhineśa havir gṛhītvā*  
*adhyātma-yoga uta yogibhir ātma-māyāṁ*  
*jijñāsubhiḥ parama-bhāgavataiḥ parīṣṭaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Sacrificers, taking oblations in their cupped hands, mediate upon you in the sacrificial fires using rules defined in the three Vedas. Similarly, the** ***yogīs,*** **absorbed in** ***ātmā,*** **meditate upon you, desiring knowledge of how to cross** ***māyā.*** **The most elevated devotees worship you with no material desires.**

**Комментарий:**

According to their qualification they worship the Lord. You are contemplated by sacrifices according to rules defined in the three Vedas. For the ignorant, various devatās are prescribed for worship. The lotus feet of the Lord give everything.

*Śruti says caraṇaṁ pavitraṁ vitataṁ purāṇaṁ yena pūtas tarati duṣkṛtāni:* we become purified and can cross *saṁsāra* by that pure, ancient foot which extended itself. *(Mahā-nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad)*

### Text 12

*paryuṣṭayā tava vibho vana-mālayeyaṁ*  
*saṁspārdhinī bhagavatī pratipatnī-vac chrīḥ*  
*yaḥ su-praṇītam amuyārhaṇam ādadan no*  
*bhūyāt sadāṅghrir aśubhāśaya-dhūmaketuḥ*

**Перевод:**

**O almighty Lord! You accept worship with the withered flower garland that we have prepared with devotion, with which Lakṣmī must compete like a jealous co-wife. May your lotus feet always act as a blazing fire to consume the inauspicious desires within our hearts!**

**Комментарий:**

This shows the great mercy of such feet given to them. Though the garland is wilted it cannot be given up by Lakṣmī, though she cannot tolerate it. Why? The explanation is given. He accepts the offering made with affection by us through the garland (*amuyā*). The Lord wears what they give from time to time. Even if the garland does not fade since it is spiritual, Lakṣmī cannot hate it since it is the decoration of the Lord. The statement is made of out of playful affection.

### Text 16

*tvattaḥ pumān samadhigamya yayāsya vīryaṁ*  
*dhatte mahāntam iva garbham amogha-vīryaḥ*  
*so ’yaṁ tayānugata ātmana āṇḍa-kośaṁ*  
*haimaṁ sasarja bahir āvaraṇair upetam*

**Перевод:**

**The original** ***puruṣa-avatāra,*** **Mahā-Viṣṇu, obtains his powers from you and establishes himself in** ***mahat-tattva,*** **the womb of the universe, through** ***māyā.*** **Then the** ***mahat-tattva,*** **obedient to** ***māyā,*** **produces from itself the golden shell of the universe with its coverings.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord as puruṣottama is superior to the jīva. But Kṛṣṇa is superior to the puruṣa who glances over prakṛti. In māyā (*yayā*) he places his śakti.

### Text 17

*tat tasthūṣaś ca jagataś ca bhavān adhīśo*  
*yan māyayottha-guṇa-vikriyayopanītān*  
*arthāñ juṣann api hṛṣīka-pate na lipto*  
*ye ’nye svataḥ parihṛtād api bibhyati sma*

**Перевод:**

**O Lord of the senses! You are the ultimate controller of all moving and nonmoving living entities since you are unaffected even though you contact the sense objects through the action of the senses, which are transformations of the** ***guṇas*** **arising from māyā. On the other hand, other living entities, even** ***yogīs*****, are frightened of the sense objects that they have renounced by their choice.**

**Комментарий:**

His power is shown. The puruṣa is your aṁśa. Because he creates he is called *īśaḥ.*

As the Lord, though you act kindly to the sense objects (*juṣan*), you are unaffected.

The result of action goes to the performer (*karma phalaṁ prayoktari*).

### Text 18

*smāyāvaloka-lava-darśita-bhāva-hāri-*  
*bhrū-maṇḍala-prahita-saurata-mantra-śauṇḍaiḥ*  
*patnyas tu ṣoḍaśa-sahasram anaṅga-bāṇair*  
*yasyendriyaṁ vimathituṁ karaṇair na vibhvyaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Sixteen thousand wives are unable to disturb your mind and senses by their lust, which is like the arrows of Cupid, by their bold messages of love sent by their eyebrows made attractive with their intentions, shown by their smiling glances.**

**Комментарий:**

“But I appear to be a person devoted to women.” That is not true. They could not disturb your senses to the extent that the women endowed with greatest prema did, by arrows of Cupid--by their gestures and emotions produced in competition with those having more prema. They could only attract him to the extent of their portion of prema. The Lord is controlled by prema. He does not have material lust.
