**Thus, in the previously mentioned places of worship and by the described methods, one should meditate on my peaceful, transcendental form with four arms holding a conch, disc, club and lotus flower, and then worship me with fixed attention.**

**Комментарий:**

**Bhakti Sandarbha 295:**

There are two types of worshipping four-handed Viṣṇu in the elements listed. One way is to worship the deity within the location by mere service to the location, just as one can clean the temple where the deity resides and thus serve the Lord. For instance, one should worship the Lord by welcoming the devotees or giving grass to the cows. It is appropriate to express friendliness to devotees but it is taught one should see the Lord as the master, for it is said:

*īsvare tad-adhīneṣu bāliśeṣu dviṣatsu ca*  
*prema-maitrī-kṛpopekṣā yaḥ karoti sa madhyamaḥ*

**An intermediate or second-class devotee, called madhyama-adhikārī*****,*** **offers his love to the Supreme Lord, is a sincere friend to all the devotees of the Lord, shows mercy to ignorant people who are innocent, and disregards those who are envious of the Lord. (SB** [**11.2.46**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/2.46)**)**

One gives grass to the cows not to the four-handed form, since the Lord does not eat grass. That is according to the following rule mentioned in the previous verse:

*yad yad iṣṭatamaṁ loke yac cāti-priyam ātmanaḥ*  
*tat tan nivedayen mahyaṁ tad ānantyāya kalpate*

**If one offers to me what is considered best according to the scriptures and to the people, and also according to one’s own preferences, one becomes qualified for eternal life. (SB** [**11.11.41**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/11.34)**)**

The other method is direct worship of the deity within the location. Thus one should think of the antaryāmī while worshipping the Lord in ether by meditating on him in the heart, or in the water by offering water to the antaryāmī there. In other instances such as fire, one should think of the antaryāmī form only. This should not be an offering to one’s cherished form of the Lord, the shelter of one’s service in prema. That form should be served with affection while thinking of him as very tender in body (not able to tolerate fire.) The Lord says:

*vastropavītābharaṇa- patra-srag-gandha-lepanaiḥ*  
*alaṅkurvīta sa-prema mad-bhakto māṁ yathocitam*

**My devotee should then lovingly decorate me with clothing, a sacred thread, various ornaments, tulasī garlands, and he should anoint my body with unguents, all in the prescribed manner. (SB** [**11.27.32**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/27.32)**)**

According to the type of bhakti of these devotees, the Lord assumes an appropriate bhāva. It is said:

*bhakti-grāhyo hṛṣīkeśo na dhanair dharaṇī-dhara \|*  
*bhaktyā saṁpūjito viṣṇuḥ pradadāti manoratham \|\|*  
*jalenāpi jagannāthaḥ pūjitaḥ kleśahā bhavet \|\|*  
*paritoṣaṁ vrajaty āśu tṛsārtaḥ sujalair yathā*

**The Lord, the support of the whole earth, is attained by bhakti, not by wealth. Worshipped with bhakti the Lord gives whatever the devotee desires. By worshipping the Lord, dispeller of all suffering, even with water, he is satisfied, just as a thirsty person becomes satisfied by clear water. (Nāradīya Purāṇa 2.3.3-4)**

In the example, a person depends on water. (Similarly the Lord’s bhāva depends on the devotee’s offering.) In opposite conditions however, offering water may be a fault. For instance worship using water is excellent in summer but detested in the rainy season.

*śuci-śukra-gate kāle ye ’rcayiṣyanti keśavam \|*  
*jalasthaṁ vividhaiḥ puṣpair mucyante yama-tāḍanāt \|\|*  
*ghanāgame prakurvanti jalasthaṁ vai janārdanam \|*  
*ye janā nṛpati-śreṣṭha teṣāṁ vai narakaṁ dhruvam \|\|*

**When the hot season has arrived those who worship the Lord with various flowers while the Lord is in the water are freed from the punishment of Yama. Those who place the Lord in water in the rainy season go to hell for certain. (Garuḍa Purāṇa)**

Elsewhere also there are hundreds of methods of pleasing the Lord according to time and place, according to rules of service. The opposite actions (which do not please the Lord) are forbidden. *Viṣṇoḥ sarva-rtu-caryām*: Viṣṇu should be served according to all the seasons. (Viṣṇu-yamala) Therefore it is said:

*yad yad iṣṭatamaṁ loke yac cāti-priyam ātmanaḥ*  
*tat tan nivedayen mahyaṁ tad ānantyāya kalpate*

**If one offers to me what is considered best according to the scriptures and to the people, and also according to one’s own preferences, one becomes qualified for eternal life. (SB** [**11.11.41**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/11.34)**)**

While meditating with the deity mantra, it is prescribed to meditate on the place, filled with attractive forms, tastes, fragrances, touch and sound of all seasons. Otherwise one’s efforts will be useless.

Thus in fire and other elements one should meditate on the form of antaryāmī only.

### Text 47

*iṣṭā-pūrtena mām evaṁ yo yajeta samāhitaḥ*  
*labhate mayi sad-bhaktiṁ mat-smṛtiḥ sādhu-sevayā*

**Перевод:**

**He who worships me with attention by sacrifices, constructing gardens and other things mentioned above obtains** ***prema-bhakti.*** **I remember him because of his great service.**

**Комментарий:**

**Bhakti Sandarbha 238:**

*Iṣṭā* refers to sacrifices like agni-hotra, darśa-paruṇamāsa, cāturmāsya, paśu-yāga, vaiśvadeva-bali-haraṇa etc. *Pūrta* refers to making temples, gardens, wells, ponds, lakes and places for drinking water. This is according to SB [7.15.48-49](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/15.48). However, sacrifices are also described in *haviṣāgnau yajeta mām*: one worships me in fire by offering oblations. (SB [11.11.43](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/11.43)) This agni-hotra represents other sacrifices as well.

Thus the real meaning should be “He who worships me by sacrifices and gardens, remembering me, attains steadiness in the essence of bhakti by devotee association.” Sacrifices are included in bhakti since one makes offerings to fire as the residence of the Lord in the form of antaryāmī. Gardens and wells are part of bhakti because they are made for serving the Lord.

### Text 48

*prāyeṇa bhakti-yogena sat-saṅgena vinoddhava*  
*nopāyo vidyate samyak prāyaṇaṁ hi satām aham*

## Chapter Twelve

## Texts 1, 2

*śrī-bhagavān uvāca*  
*na rodhayati māṁ yogo na sāṅkhyaṁ dharma eva ca*  
*na svādhyāyas tapas tyāgo neṣṭā-pūrtaṁ na dakṣiṇā*  
*vratāni yajñaś chandāṁsi tīrthāni niyamā yamāḥ*  
*yathāvarundhe sat-saṅgaḥ sarva-saṅgāpaho hi mām*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord said: O Uddhava! Only by associating with my pure devotees one can destroy material attachment and attain me. One cannot attain me by** ***aṣṭāṅga-yoga,*** **distinction of** ***ātmā*** **from body, practice of nonviolence, study of the Vedas, austerity,** ***sannyāsa,*** **sacrifices, charitable projects, donations, vows, worship of** ***devatās*****, secret** ***mantras*****, holy places, or observing prohibitions and rules. (SB** [**11.12.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.1)**)**

**Комментарий:**

**Bhakti Sandarbha 238:**

There are two types of service rendered to the mahā-bhāgavata: prasaṅga-rūpa (associating, hearing), paricaryā-rūpa (rending physical service). Prasaṅga-rūpa service is as follows:

*na rodhayati māṁ yogo na sāṅkhyaṁ dharma eva ca*  
*na svādhyāyas tapas tyāgo neṣṭā-pūrtaṁ na dakṣiṇā*  
*vratāni yajñaś chandāṁsi tīrthāni niyamā yamāḥ*  
*yathāvarundhe sat-saṅgaḥ sarva-saṅgāpaho hi mām*

**O Uddhava! Only by associating with my pure devotees one can destroy material attachment and attain me. One cannot attain me by aṣṭāṅga-yoga, distinction of ātmā from body, practice of nonviolence, study of the Vedas, austerity, sannyāsa, sacrifices, charitable projects, donations, vows, worship of devatās, secret mantras, holy places, or observing prohibitions and rules. (SB** [**11.12.1-2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.1)**)**

In order to explain the great power of devotee association on which all else depends, the Lord teaches the greatest secret:

*athaitat paramaṁ guhyaṁ śṛṇvato yadu-nandana*  
*su-gopyam api vakṣyāmi tvaṁ me bhṛtyaḥ suhṛt sakhā*

**O beloved of the Yadu dynasty! Because you are my servant, well-wisher and friend, I shall now speak to you who listen well the supreme secret. (SB** [**11.11.49**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/11.49)**)**

Because no other process has such power, devotee association is called the greatest secret. This is then explained in SB 11.12.1-2 (quoted above).

*na rodhayati māṁ yogo na sāṅkhyaṁ dharma eva ca*  
*na svādhyāyas tapas tyāgo neṣṭā-pūrtaṁ na dakṣiṇā*  
*vratāni yajñaś chandāṁsi tīrthāni niyamā yamāḥ*  
*yathāvarundhe sat-saṅgaḥ sarva-saṅgāpaho hi mām*

**O Uddhava! Only by associating with my pure devotees one can destroy material attachment and attain me. One cannot attain me by aṣṭāṅga-yoga, distinction of ātmā from body, practice of nonviolence, study of the Vedas, austerity, sannyāsa, sacrifices, charitable projects, donations, vows, worship of devatās, secret mantras, holy places, or observing prohibitions and rules. (SB** [**11.12.1-2**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.1)**)**

*Tyāgaḥ* means sannyāsa. *Dakṣināḥ* means charity work. *Yajñaḥ* means deity worship. *Chandāṁsi* means secret mantras. Yoga and other processes do not bring me under control as much as association with devotees brings me under control. This indicates that the elements in the list control the Lord to some extent. That means that these elements are connected with the Lord, and are not the ordinary yoga, sannyāsa, or charity. Thus *vratāni* means Ekādaśī and other devotional fasts according to the commentator.

Because association is so pleasing to the Lord, this does not mean that one does not have to perform the regular Vaiṣṇava vratas, since glorifying the power of one action of bhakti to give results does not negate the obligation to perform other actions regularly.

*na hy agni-mukhato ’yaṁ vai bhagavān sarva-yajña-bhuk*  
*ijyeta haviṣā rājan yathā vipra-mukhe hutaiḥ*

**O King! The Supreme Lord, enjoyer of all sacrifices, is not worshipped by offerings to the fires as much as he is worshiped by offerings to the mouths of the brāhmaṇas*****.*** **(SB** [**7.14.17**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/14.17)**)**

Hearing this, the followers of karma should not give up fire sacrifices. Similarly the devotees hearing that one should worship the devotee profusely (*mad-bhakta-pūjādhyadhikā* SB [11.19.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/19.20)) should not give up worshipping the Lord which is prescribed as a regular practice after taking dīkṣā.

*ṣaḍbhir māsopavāsais tu yat phalaṁ parikīrtitam \|*  
*viṣṇor naivedya-sikthena tat phalaṁ bhuñjatāṁ kalau \|\|*

**The glorified results from doing six months fasting are attained in Kali-yuga by taking a mouthful of food offered to Viṣṇu. (Skanda Purāṇa)**

Such a statement does not negate the rule to observe fasting on Ekādaśī which is also part of bhakti, and which also gives great results. Therefore one must observe such Vaiṣṇava vratas, even just to maintain their regular performance. Regular Vaiṣṇava vratas will later be discussed in relation to arcana. Thus Śrīdhara Svāmī, commenting on giving up all dharmas and worshipping the Lord in SB [11.11.32](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/11.29) says “One gives up dharmas contrary to bhakti such as offering śrāddha food which is not Viṣṇu prasāda, observing viddha ekādaśī or not observing ekādaśī during the waning phase of the moon.” Performance of bhagavad-dharma is mentioned by Bhīṣma in the first Canto. The commentary says, “This means following rules for Dvādaśī etc. for the pleasure of the Lord.” In the commentary on *vratāni cere hari-toṣaṇāni* (he performed austerities for the satisfaction of the Lord. SB [3.1.19](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/1.19)) Śrīdhara Svāmī says, “Vratas means Ekādaśī etc.”

The necessity of observing many aṅgas of bhakti (not just engaging in devotee association) is confirmed by conduct of Ambarīṣa, crest jewel among devotees, who vowed to eat only food offered to the Lord (and performed many other aṅgas of bhakti).

*vaiṣṇavo yadi bhuñjīta ekādaśyāṁ pramādataḥ \|*  
*viṣṇv-arcanaṁ vṛthā tasya narakaṁ ghoram āpnuyāt \|\|*

**One should not eat on the eleventh day of the waxing and waning moons. One should stay awake on that night and worship the Lord with special attention. (Gautamīya-tantra)**

### Text 3-6

*sat-saṅgena hi daiteyā yātudhānā mṛgāḥ khagāḥ*  
*gandharvāpsaraso nāgāḥ siddhāś cāraṇa-guhyakāḥ*  
*vidyādharā manuṣyeṣu vaiśyāḥ śūdrāḥ striyo ’ntya-jāḥ*  
*rajas-tamaḥ-prakṛtayas tasmiṁs tasmin yuge yuge*  
*bahavo mat-padaṁ prāptās tvāṣṭra-kāyādhavādayaḥ*  
*vṛṣaparvā balir bāṇo mayaś cātha vibhīṣaṇaḥ*  
*sugrīvo hanumān ṛkṣo gajo gṛdhro vaṇikpathaḥ*  
*vyādhaḥ kubjā vraje gopyo yajña-patnyas tathāpare*

**Перевод:**

**In every** ***yuga*** **many living entities entangled in the modes of passion and ignorance gained the association of my devotees. Thus, such living entities as the Daityas, Rākṣasas, birds, beasts, Gandharvas, Apsarās, Nāgas, Siddhas, Cāraṇas, Guhyakas and Vidyādharas, as well as such lower-class human beings as the** ***vaiśyas, śūdras*****, women and others, were able to achieve my supreme abode. Vṛtrāsura, Prahlāda Mahārāja and others like them also achieved my abode by association with my devotees, as did personalities such as Vṛṣaparvā, Bali Mahārāja, Bāṇāsura, Maya, Vibhīṣaṇa, Sugrīva, Hanumān, Jāmbavān, Gajendra, Jaṭāyu, Tulādhāra, Dharma-vyādha, Kubjā, the** ***gopīs*** **in Vṛndāvana and the wives of the** ***brāhmaṇas*** **who were performing sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

**Bhakti Sandarbha 239:**

Now we return to the subject. Controlling the Lord (mentioned in SB [11.12.1-2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.1)) has two types: primary and secondary. By primary control one attains prema.

*rājan patir gurur alaṁ bhavatāṁ yadūnāṁ*  
*daivaṁ priyaḥ kula-patiḥ kva ca kiṅkaro vaḥ*  
*astv evam aṅga bhagavān bhajatāṁ mukundo*  
*muktiṁ dadāti karhicit sma na bhakti-yogam*

**O King Parīkṣit! Kṛṣṇa was the protector, instructor, object of worship, giver of affection, and the protector of both the Yadus and Pāṇḍavas. But he was sometimes your servant. The Lord gives liberation but not even bhāva-bhakti to those who worship him. (SB** [**5.6.18**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/6.18)**)**

By secondary control over the Lord one attains results other than prema. An example of primary control is the gopīs. An example of secondary control is Bāṇa. Secondary control over the Lord makes the Lord inclined to give benedictions. Here are some examples of gaining control of the Lord:

*sat-saṅgena hi daiteyā yātudhānā mṛgāḥ khagāḥ*  
*gandharvāpsaraso nāgāḥ siddhāś cāraṇa-guhyakāḥ*  
*vidyādharā manuṣyeṣu vaiśyāḥ śūdrāḥ striyo ’ntya-jāḥ*  
*rajas-tamaḥ-prakṛtayas tasmiṁs tasmin yuge yuge*  
*bahavo mat-padaṁ prāptās tvāṣṭra-kāyādhavādayaḥ*  
*vṛṣaparvā balir bāṇo mayaś cātha vibhīṣaṇaḥ*  
*sugrīvo hanumān ṛkṣo gajo gṛdhro vaṇikpathaḥ*  
*vyādhaḥ kubjā vraje gopyo yajña-patnyas tathāpare*

**In every yuga many living entities entangled in the modes of passion and ignorance gained the association of my devotees. Thus, such living entities as the Daityas, Rākṣasas, birds, beasts, Gandharvas, Apsarās, Nāgas, Siddhas, Cāraṇas, Guhyakas and Vidyādharas, as well as such lower-class human beings as the vaiśyas, śūdras, women and others, were able to achieve my supreme abode. Vṛtrāsura, Prahlāda Mahārāja and others like them also achieved my abode by association with my devotees, as did personalities such as Vṛṣaparvā, Bali Mahārāja, Bāṇāsura, Maya, Vibhīṣaṇa, Sugrīva, Hanumān, Jāmbavān, Gajendra, Jaṭāyu, Tulādhāra, Dharma-vyādha, Kubjā, the gopīsin Vṛndāvana and the wives of the brāhmaṇas who were performing sacrifice. (SB** [**11.12.3-6**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.3)**)**

*Daiteyā* also means Dānavas and other demons. *Yātudhānā* means Rākṣasas. Some examples are given. Tvāṣṭrā was the demon Vṛtra. In his previous life he had association with Nārada, Aṅgirā and Saṅkarṣaṇa. Kāyādhavaḥ means Prahlāda, the son of Kayādhu. In the womb he had Nārada’s association. The word *ādayaḥ* indicates that examples of persons mentioned in the previous list are given in order. Vṛṣa-parvā was a Dānava. As soon as he was born he was given up by his mother and raised by a sage. He became a devotee of Viṣṇu. This is stated in another Purāṇa.

Bali had association of Prahlāda and Vāmana. Only after that association did his bhakti manifest. Bāṇa had association of Bali, Śiva and the Lord. After his arms were severed he recognized the greatness of Viṣṇu and he attained Śiva, a great devotee of the Lord (rather than the Lord). Attaining the devotee is included in *mat-padaṁ prāptāḥ:* they attained me. Maya was a Dānava. He attained association of the Pāṇḍavas and the Lord when he built the assembly hall. Later he attained the Lord. Vibhīṣaṇa was a Yātudhāna. He had association of Hanumān and the Lord.

Sugrīva, Hanumān, Ṛkṣa and Gajendra are animals. Ṛkṣa means Jāmbavān. He had the Lord’s association. Gajendra in his previous life had devotee association. In the next life he attained the Lord’s association. Gṛdhdra refers to Jaṭāyu who was a bird. He had the association of Garuḍa and Daśaratha. He saw Sītā and Rāma. Examples of Gandharvas and others are not given since they are not too well known. Among humans, the vaiśya is illustrated with Tulādhāra, a merchant. In Mahābhārata he is inferred to have had devotee association and is glorified in relation to the story of Jājāli and a Gandharva. Vyādha means Dharma-vyāda who was a śūdra or antyaja (outcaste).

The story is found in Ādivarāha Purāṇa. In a previous kali-yuga, in a previous life, a brāhmaṇa was killed by a king named Vasu, who was a devotee, mistaking him for a deer. The brāhmaṇa later became a brahma-rākṣasa. When the king attained Vaikuṇṭha in the material world, that rākṣasa entered his body. When the King returned to earth after his enjoyment was finished and attained a king’s body again, the rākṣasa was released from his body by the power of the king’s recitation of a prayer called Brahma-pāra. The king named him Dharma-vyādha. He was non-violent and at the end of his life, seeing Jagannātha, he praised him. Embraced by the Lord he merged with the Lord.

Kubjā attained the Lord’s association and in previous life she had Nārada’s association. This is known from Māthura-hari-vaṁśa. The gopīs mentioned here are ordinary gopīs (not the nitya-siddhas). Through marriage they came to Vraja. There they attained association of the nitya-siddha gopīs, and attained Kṛṣṇa’s association in the form of seeing him etc. The wives of the brāhmaṇas had association of reciters who narrated the qualities of Kṛṣṇa and later attained his association. The word *apare* at the end of the verse indicates the sons of Diti and others.

### Text 7

*te nādhīta-śruti-gaṇā nopāsita-mahattamāḥ*  
*avratātapta-tapasaḥ mat-saṅgān mām upāgatāḥ*

**Перевод:**

**The persons I have mentioned did not undergo serious studies of the Vedic literature, nor did they worship great saintly persons, nor did they execute severe vows or austerities. Simply by association with my devotees, they achieved me.**

**Комментарий:**

**Bhakti Sandarbha 240:**

Without any other sādhana except association, the persons in the above list attained the Lord. They did not study the śrutis (*nādhītāḥ).* Consequently they did not worship great saints. They did not do any vratas or austerities. These acts are for pleasing the Lord but they did not perform them. Sādhana performed in a previous life in the case of Vṛtra and others was also a result of previous association of devotees. Dharma-vyāda and others however attained perfection only by association.

*Mat-saṅgāt* means from association with me or with my devotees. In either case there is a relationship with the Lord*. Sat-saṅga* also means association with the Lord since the Lord is also *sat* or saintly. Previously it was said that only by association of devotees can one attain the Lord’s mercy. This means that the person becomes inclined to the Lord. In the above verses it is stated that the association of devotees is a special sādhana. It is not a fault if association with the Lord is included in that. Though sometimes the Lord’s association is the cause of developing a favorable attitude to the Lord, it should be explained as follows. When the Lord sometimes appears as avatāra, gives his association and spreads mercy everywhere directly, that takes place in relation to devotees. (He appears mainly to please the devotees.) That is the meaning of *sat* (devotee). Thus there is no contradiction.

An example of primary control of the Lord is the gopīs and others, who had association of the Lord alone, without any other means of attaining control over him.

*Bhāvena* means “by prīti which arose only by association with me.” Bhāva is a symptom of primary control.

*mayi nirbaddha-hṛdayāḥ sādhavaḥ sama-darśanāḥ*  
*vaśe kurvanti māṁ bhaktyā sat-striyaḥ sat-patiṁ yathā*

**As chaste women bring their gentle husbands under control by service, the pure devotees, who see others’ suffering as their own and are completely attached to me in the core of the heart, bring me under their full control. (SB** [**9.4.66**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-9/4.66)**)**

*bhaktyāham ekayā grāhyaḥ śraddhayātmā priyaḥ satām*  
*bhaktiḥ punāti man-niṣṭhā śva-pākān api sambhavāt*

**Only by bhakti with full faith can the devotees obtain me, Paramātmā, the object of love. Bhakti fixed in me purifies even a dog eater of his low birth. (SB** [**11.14.21**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/14.21)**)**

These cows, like these gopīs (not the nitya-siddha gopīs), were new comers to Vraja. Trees were Yamalārjuna trees. Animals were previously mentioned. Snakes were Kāliya and others. The attainment of Yamalārjuna trees and Kāliya was momentary during Kṛṣṇa’s earthly pastimes, but it should be understood that later they attained the Lord permanently.

They attained perfection *(siddhāḥ*) by either association of the Lord or his devotees. They could not attain bhāva by yoga or other means. *Yathāvarundhe sat-saṅgaḥ:* other processes cannot attain me as much as association of devotees does. (SB [11.12.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.1)) *Yathā* indicates that association leads to the highest attainment, bhāva.

### Text 9

*yaṁ na yogena sāṅkhyena dāna-vrata-tapo-’dhvaraiḥ*  
*vyākhyā-svādhyāya-sannyāsaiḥ prāpnuyād yatnavān api*

**Перевод:**

**But I cannot be attained by intense efforts of** ***yoga*****, Sāṅkhya, charity, vows, austerity, sacrifices, explaining the Vedas, study of the Vedas, or** ***sannyāsa.***

**Комментарий:**

**Bhakti Sandarbha 242:**

One does not attain that bhāva (*yam*) by yoga etc. even if they are linked to the Lord, even if those methods are executed with effort. From this effort it is understood that these methods are employed to attain the Lord. Among those who attained the Lord, the gopīs, are the most exalted. This can be understood from other sections of the work. That they attained the highest position is indicated as the greatest secret:

*athaitat paramaṁ guhyaṁ śṛṇvato yadu-nandana*  
*su-gopyam api vakṣyāmi tvaṁ me bhṛtyaḥ suhṛt sakhā*

**O beloved of the Yadu dynasty! Because you are my servant, well-wisher and friend, I shall now speak to you who listen well the supreme secret. (SB** [**11.11.49**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/11.49)**)**

### Text 10

*rāmeṇa sārdhaṁ mathurāṁ praṇīte*  
*śvāphalkinā mayy anurakta-cittāḥ*  
*vigāḍha-bhāvena na me viyoga-*  
*tīvrādhayo ’nyaṁ dadṛśuḥ sukhāya*

**Перевод:**

**The** ***gopīs*** **were always completely attached to me with the deepest love. Therefore, when my uncle Akrūra brought my brother Balarāma and me to the city of Mathurā, suffering extreme mental distress because of separation from me, they could not find any other source of happiness.**

**Комментарий:**

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 175:**

Filled with intense pain from separation because of deep love, they did not see any person other than me, such as their own sakhīs, as a source of happiness (in the past). This means that *now* they see happiness. They have no separation.

### Text 11

*tās tāḥ kṣapāḥ preṣṭhatamena nītā*  
*mayaiva vṛndāvana-gocareṇa*  
*kṣaṇārdha-vat tāḥ punar aṅga tāsāṁ*  
*hīnā mayā kalpa-samā babhūvuḥ*

**Перевод:**

**All of those nights that the** ***gopīs*** **spent with me, their most dearly beloved, in the land of Vṛndāvana while I herded the cows, seemed to them to pass in less than a moment. Bereft of my association, however, the** ***gopīs*** **felt that those same nights were equal to a day of Brahmā.**

**Комментарий:**

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 175:**

All the nights being without me were like a kalpa. But now those nights are not so. There is no separation now. Previously Kṛṣṇa said to Uddhava using the present tense:

*mayi tāḥ preyasāṁ preṣṭhe dūra-sthe gokula-striyaḥ*  
*smarantyo ’ṅga vimuhyanti virahautkaṇṭhya-vihvalāḥ*

**My dear Uddhava, for those women of Gokula I am the most cherished object of love. Thus when they remember me, who am so far away, they are overwhelmed by the anxiety of separation.**

*dhārayanty ati-kṛcchreṇa prāyaḥ prāṇān kathañcana*  
*pratyāgamana-sandeśair ballavyo me mad-ātmikāḥ*

**Simply because I have promised to return to them, my fully devoted cowherd girlfriends struggle to maintain their lives somehow or other. (SB** [**10.46.5-6**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/46.5)**)**

In this way the meaning is understood (there is no longer any separation).

### Text 12

*tā nāvidan mayy anuṣaṅga-baddha-*  
*dhiyaḥ svam ātmānam adas tathedam*  
*yathā samādhau munayo ’bdhi-toye*  
*nadyaḥ praviṣṭā iva nāma-rūpe*

**Перевод:**

**Their minds bound by constant association with me, they were not aware of their bodies, of this world or the next world, just as sages in** ***samādhi*** **are not aware of the world, and just as rivers, on entering the ocean, lose their names and forms.**

**Комментарий:**

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 176:**

Not distinguishing between the prakaṭa and aprakaṭa pastimes, and attaining aprakaṭa pastimes, the gopīs remained in this world as names and forms only (since their minds were with Kṛṣṇa.

They (*tāḥ*) remained in a state of mahābhāva, difficult to bear, manifested by extreme longing due to separation. Their intelligence was bound up by union, for they attained my association as I came to see them, attaining a meeting in a state of mahā-modana. They were not aware of a sense of possession (*svam)* or identity (*ātmānam)* and did not know if these were aprakaṭa pastimes or prakaṭa pastimes. They understood them as one.

There is a difference in prakaṭa and aprakaṭa: two manifestations, two identities,and two pastimes. But they recognized them as non-different. They thus seemed to merge into (*praviṣṭāḥ iva*) the aprakaṭa manifestation with its name and form (*nāma-rūpe*) but actually did not, since the entities are non-different to begin with. *Nāma-rūpa* is a compound.

They seemed to enter into an identity with him. This indicates there was actual difference, just as the ātmās of the sages, endowed with waking, dreaming and deep sleep states because of upādhis, seem to enter into the tūrīya, the state of samādhi. But the fourth state is spread in the other three states. The difference arises by upādhis. Giving up the upādhis, it appears that these states enter the fourth state. The gopīs gave up everything which were obstacles to attaining Kṛṣṇa.

They seemed to enter into state of possessiveness. This was done partially. Just as rivers seem to enter an ocean, they seemed to enter a state where they could not distinguish different between the prakaṭa and aprakaṭa states.

### Text 13

*mat-kāmā ramaṇaṁ jāram asvarūpa-vido ’balāḥ*  
*brahma māṁ paramaṁ prāpuḥ saṅgāc chata-sahasraśaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**All those hundreds of thousands of** ***gopīs,*** **desiring me, the supreme Brahman, not knowing my form of power, attained me, a lover who gave them pleasure because of association.**

**Комментарий:**

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 177:**

The cause of the gopīs attaining Kṛṣṇa in both prakaṭa and aprakaṭa pastimes is their bhāva. Having entered the aprakaṭa pastimes, they attained his svarūpa. The meaning is this. In the statement “He glances at great, attractive Bhīṣma (*bhīṣmam udāraṁ darśanīyam kaṭaṁ karoti*) the phrase *kaṭaṁ karoti*, formed of verb (*karoti)* and object *(kaṭam*) acts as a verb (*kaṭaṁ karoti*) with Bhīṣma as the object. Similarly *brahma prāpuḥ* should be taken as a verb (they attained Brahman) with *paramam* and *ramaṇaṁ jāram* as its objects. Thus the meaning is “They attained the Brahman, a supreme form, who is the supremely attractive lover.” The descriptive terms are non-different from the object Brahman because the supreme object is the shelter of all descriptive elements. *Ekaṁ evādvitīyaṁ brahma*: Brahman is one without a second. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [6.1.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chandogya/6.1.11)) Though the descriptive terms are non-different from Brahman, each one has difference. *Sa ekadhā bhavati dvidhā bhavati*: he is one and becomes two. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 10.24.26) The order of words is significant also. The emphasis is on the first phrase *ramaṇaṁ jāram* as in a sentence like the following where the first phrase *pacyantāṁ vividhāḥ pākāḥ* has most importance:

*pacyantāṁ vividhāḥ pākāḥ sūpāntāḥ pāyasādayaḥ*  
*saṁyāvāpūpa-śaṣkulyaḥ sarva-dohaś ca gṛhyatām*

**Let many different kinds of food be cooked, from sweet rice to vegetable soups! Many kinds of fancy cakes, both baked and fried, should be prepared. And all the available milk products should be taken for this sacrifice. (SB** [**10.24.26**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/24.26)**)**

Another example would be *agni-hotraṁ juhoti yavāgaṁ pacati*: perform the sacrifice and cook the barley.

According to what was explained, the meaning of *tā abalā brahma prāpuḥ paramam* is “The girls attained Brahman, but its supreme form (*paramam*), Bhagavān.” *Brahmaṇo hi pratiṣhāham*: I am the basis of Brahman. (BG [14.27](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/14.27)) In that condition, others, hundreds of thousands of gopīs in Gokula, attained Kṛṣṇa from association with his eternal consorts, who are his aṁśas. They attained him by association with the eternal consorts because that association quickly generates a similar bhāva. It was said previously *kevalena hi bhāvena gopyo gāvaḥ*: by intense bhāva the gopīs and cows attained him. (SB [11.12.8-9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.8)) What is he called? They attained me, Kṛṣṇa, who is supreme Brahman in human form.

*yo’vatārāṇāṁ madhye śreṣṭho’vatāraḥ ko bhavati \|*  
*yena lokās tuṣṭā devās tuṣṭā bhavanti \|*  
*yaṁ smṛtvā muktā asmāt saṁsārād bhavanti \|*  
*kathaṁ vāsyāvatārasya brahmatā bhavati \|*

**Brahmā spoke. Who is the best among the avatāras, by whom all people and devatās become satisfied, by whose remembrance people become liberated from saṁsāra? How can this avatāra be Svayam Bhagavān? (Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad** [**2.24**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/2.24)**)**

What was the relationship with you that they attained? They attained *ramaṇam jāram*, a husband, since the etymological meaning is rejected as in the case of *nandana,* whose common meaning is son, though literally it means “one who gives joy.” Etymologically *ramaṇa* (one who gives pleasure) and *nandana* (one who gives joy) have similar meanings. *Mitrā-putra* means *mitrā-nandana* and not *mitrā-pati*. *Mitrā-pati* means *mitrā-ramaṇa* and not *mitrā-putra.* The word *jāra* should have a different meaning (*ramaṇa* and *jāra* have the same meaning if *ramaṇa* is not taken as husband) since different words in a list should indicate different things. It cannot be *jāra* or a paramour. That is only a perception, since the meaning should be different as in the phrase *gopīnām tat-patīnām:* the gopīs and their husbands (who were apparent husbands only, since Kṛṣṇa is their real husband).He must be the husband since they are all aṁśas of the supreme Brahman and he is the protector of all (*pati*). In this way Piṅgalā said:

*santuṣṭā śraddadhaty etad yathā-lābhena jīvatī*  
*viharāmy amunaivāham ātmanā ramaṇena vai*

**Satisfied, having faith in the Lord and maintaining myself with whatever comes of its own accord, I shall enjoy life with only the Lord, because he is the real source of love and happiness. (SB** [**11.8.40**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/8.40)**)**

He whom they attained previously as an illicit lover they later attained in his true form. Thus Kṛṣṇa as paramour (*jāra*) is not ultimate but Kṛṣṇa as their husband (*ramaṇa*) isthe ultimate form.Being a husband *(ramaṇa)* in itself does not indicate Kṛṣna, Bhagavān. Thus the word *mām* (me) is included in the verse. The word *mām* is then explained by using the word Brahman. This is another proof that Brahman means Bhagavān.

One may argue that since the word *ramaṇa* comes before *jāra* the perception as husband comes before the perception of paramour. This has to be refuted by various arguments. Unlike the order of *brahma māṁ paramam,* the word *jāram* should be placed first since the state of being a paramour occurs before being a husband. This is the strongest argument of all. Thus the meaning cannot be that he was seen as a husband and then as a paramour. In this way scripture becomes meaningful. For instance in the phrase *dadhnā juhoti:* sacrifice using yogurt, the principle element is the injunction to perform sacrifice and the accessory is the yogurt. Thus the sequence starts from *ramaṇa*being unaccomplished (as *jāra)* and ends with *mām paramam.*

*Nanda-gopa-sutaṁ devi patiṁ me kuru te namaḥ:* I offer respects to the son of Nanda, and our husband. (SB [10.22.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/22.4)) Being the husband is the complete condition for the gopīs. This determination of the gopīs with spotless love should be fulfilled by the Lord. He personally accepted them. Their desire was fulfilled.

*yātābalā vrajaṁ siddhā mayemā raṁsyathā kṣapāḥ*  
*yad uddiśya vratam idaṁ cerur āryārcanaṁ satīḥ*

**Go now, girls, and return to Vraja. Your desire is fulfilled, for in my company you will enjoy the coming nights. After all, this was the purpose of your vow to worship goddess Kātyāyanī, O pure-hearted ones. (SB** [**10.22.27**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/22.27)**)**

In all cases the desired attainment defined as ultimate should agree with the conclusions of rasa scriptures. All ancient and modern scholars, mundane and spiritual have described it in this way (marriage as the conclusion). Rūpa Gosvāmī also in the act called “Fulfillment of Desires” in Lalita-mādhava has shown this and in Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi has concluded this in the stage of *samṛrdhi-sambhoga*, complete enjoyment, full of all rasa.

This is also indicated in Bhāgavatam.

*yat tv ahaṁ bhavatīnāṁ vai dūre varte priyo dṛśām*  
*manasaḥ sannikarṣārthaṁ mad-anudhyāna-kāmyayā*

**But the actual reason why I, your beloved, have stayed far away from your eyes is that I wanted to intensify my meditation upon you and thus draw my mind closer to you. (SB** [**10.47.34**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/47.34)**)**

*yā mayā krīḍatā rātryāṁ vane ’smin vraja āsthitāḥ*  
*alabdha-rāsāḥ kalyāṇyo māpur mad-vīrya-cintayā*

**O fortunate ladies! Although some gopīs had to remain in the cowherd village and so could not join the rāsa dance to sport with me at night in the forest, they attained me by thinking of my potent pastimes. (SB** [**10.47.37**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/47.37)**)**

Meeting in an illicit way is always filled with obstacles. Therefore it is not approved in rasa scriptures as the ultimate spiritual goal. The millions of gopīs in Vraja filled with mahābhāva found it most difficult previously to hide their feelings.

*nija-padābja-dalair dhvaja-vajra*  
*nīrajāṅkuśa-vicitra-lalāmaiḥ*  
*vraja-bhuvaḥ śamayan khura-todaṁ*  
*varṣma-dhurya-gatir īḍita-veṇuḥ*  
*vrajati tena vayaṁ sa-vilāsa*  
*vīkṣaṇārpita-manobhava-vegāḥ*  
*kuja-gatiṁ gamitā na vidāmaḥ*  
*kaśmalena kavaraṁ vasanaṁ vā*

**As Kṛṣṇa strolls through Vraja with his lotus-petal-like feet, marking the ground with the distinctive emblems of flag, thunderbolt, lotus and elephant goad, he relieves the distress the ground feels from the cows’ hooves. As he plays his renowned flute, his body moves slowly with the grace of an elephant. Thus we gopīs, who become agitated by Cupid when Kṛṣṇa playfully glances at us, stand as still as trees, unaware that our hair and garments are slackening. (SB** [**10.35.16-17**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/35.16)**)**

In long separation the feelings became intense.

*nivārayāmaḥ samupetya mādhavaṁ*  
*kiṁ no ’kariṣyan kula-vṛddha-bāndhavāḥ*

**Let us directly approach Mādhava and stop him from going. What can our family elders and other relatives do to us? (SB** [**10.39.28**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/39.28)**)**

*visṛjya lajjāṁ ruruduḥ sma su-svaraṁ*  
*govinda dāmodara mādhaveti*

**They forgot all shame and loudly cried out, “O Govinda! O Dāmodara! O Mādhava!” (SB** [**10.39.31**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/39.31)**)**

*tā man-manaskā mat-prāṇā mad-arthe tyakta-daihikāḥ*

**Those gopīs are always absorbed in me, and their very lives are ever devoted to me. (SB** [**10.46.4**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/46.4)**)**

*iti gopyo hi govinde gata-vāk-kāya-mānasāḥ*  
*kṛṣṇa-dūte samāyāte uddhave tyakta-laukikāḥ*  
*gāyantyaḥ prīya-karmāṇi rudantyaś ca gata-hriyaḥ*  
*tasya saṁsmṛtya saṁsmṛtya yāni kaiśora-bālyayoḥ*

**Thus speaking, the gopīs, whose words, bodies and minds were fully dedicated to Govinda, put aside all their regular work now that Kṛṣṇa’s messenger, Uddhava, had arrived among them. Constantly remembering the activities their beloved Kṛṣṇa had performed in his childhood and youth, they sang about them and cried without shame. (SB** [**10.47.9-10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/47.9)**)**

*kācin madhukaraṁ dṛṣṭvā dhyāyantī kṛṣṇa-saṅgamam*  
*priya-prasthāpitaṁ dūtaṁ kalpayitvedam abravīt*

**One of the gopīs, while meditating on her previous association with Kṛṣṇa, saw a honeybee before her and imagined it to be a messenger sent by her beloved. Thus she spoke as follows. (SB** [**10.47.11**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/47.11)**)**

*yā dustyajaṁ sva-janam ārya-pathaṁ ca hitvā*  
*bhejur mukunda-padavīṁ śrutibhir vimṛgyām*

**The gopīs of Vṛndāvana have given up the association of their husbands, sons and other family members, who are very difficult to give up, and they have forsaken their relatives and the path of dharma to obtain the lotus feet of Mukunda, Kṛṣṇa, which even the Vedas pursue. (SB** [**10.47.61**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/47.61)**)**

*gopyo hasantyaḥ papracchū rāma-sandarśanādṛtāḥ*  
*kaccid āste sukhaṁ kṛṣṇaḥ pura-strī-jana-vallabhaḥ*

**Honored to have the personal audience of Balarāma, the young gopīs*****,*** **smiled and asked him, “Is Kṛṣṇa, the darling of the city women, living happily? (SB** [**10.65.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/65.9)**)**

*mātaraṁ pitaraṁ bhrātṝn patīn putrān svasṝn api*  
*yad-arthe jahima dāśārha dustyajān sva-janān prabho*

**For Kṛṣṇa’s sake, O descendant of Daśārha, we abandoned our mothers, fathers, brothers, husbands, children and sisters, even though these family relations are difficult to give up. (SB** [**10.65.11**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/65.11)**)**

They were determined to stop Kṛṣṇa from going, giving up all shame. They gave up shame because of the appearance of their bhāva, and since all people of Gokula were crying at that time. They thought they could stop him by crying together in front of him. Giving up their household chores shows clearly the appearance of their bhāva. More than that, they gave up their parents and relatives. This was not just the first stirring of love (*pūrva-rāga*) as expressed in SB [10.31.16](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/31.16). Their laughing indicates a condition of madness (*unmāda*) since it was not suitable to laugh at that time. Looking at the bee also is a sign of madness. The unlimited number of gopīs, acting insanely, giving up shame and giving up parents, could not hide their bhāva, because it was uncontrollable mahābhāva. Though this became known, it remained as if unknown by everyone because of everyone’s great pain of separation from Kṛṣṇa. They thought about this only later. By hiding their bhāva for some time, they could enjoy. And when it was finally revealed, it appeared dharmic in actuality for them and others. This is the opinion of the knowers of rasa. Rasa seen as lacking in dharma is destroyed by its vulgarity.

Rasa with adharma is of two types: parakīya (relationship with a woman dependent on another –a husband) and para-sparśa (relationship with a woman touched by another man). In the assembly of Parīkṣit, which was filled with aiśvarya-jñāna, adharmic love was rejected and rasa was produced according to explanations of aiśvarya-jñāna (they were eternally married to Kṛṣṇa in aprakaṭa pastimes). In the assembly of Gokula filled with prema the problem was resolved in order to act according to earthly vision (getting married), taking support of *lokavat līlā kaivalya*: the Lord performs actions as pastimes, absorbed in them like a person of this world. (Brahma-sūtra [2.1.33](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/2.1.33))

It is also said:

*nāsūyan khalu kṛṣṇāya mohitās tasya māyayā*  
*manyamānāḥ sva-pārśva-sthānv svān svān dārān vrajaukasaḥ*

**The cowherd men, bewildered by Kṛṣṇa’s yogamāyā, thought their wives had remained home at their sides. They did not harbor any jealous feelings against him at any time. (SB** [**10.33.37**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/33.37)**)**

This statement should also be correlated. Bewildered by his māyā, they did not hate Kṛṣṇa: they did not find fault in Kṛṣṇa. How can Kṛṣṇa, the very life of our dharma, artha, friends, wives, sons and life airs act wrongly by accepting another’s wife? The māyā is described. They thought their wives were by their sides but these wives were made by māyā in order avoid the event that the wives of the Lord, with spiritual bodies, would be oppressed by another person. This confirms our proposal.

This is also a solution to the following situation:

*antar-gṛha-gatāḥ kāścid gopyo ’labdha-vinirgamāḥ*  
*kṛṣṇaṁ tad-bhāvanā-yuktā dadhyur mīlita-locanāḥ*

**Some of the gopīs, however, could not manage to get out of their houses, and instead they remained home with eyes closed, meditating upon him in pure love. (SB** [**10.29.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/29.9)**)**

His māyā, capable of anything, starting from the time they were married to their apparent husbands, made arrangements of false forms for his dear gopīs who had attraction only for him in order to protect their morality, since his māyā is most careful in these matters. This particular day is representative of other days as well.

In Kūrma Purāṇa, Thirty-second Chapter, it is shown that a chaste woman cannot be defeated by another person. Sītā is given as an example.

*pati-vratādharma-parā rudrāṇy eva na saṁśayaḥ \|*  
*nāsyāṁ parābhāvaṁ kartuṁ śaknotīha janaḥ kvacit \|\|*  
*yathā rāmasya subhagā sītā trailoka-viśrutā \|*  
*patnī dāśarather devī vijigye rākṣaseśvaram \|\|*  
*rāmasya bhāryāṁ vimalāṁ rāvaṇo rākṣaseśvaraḥ \|*  
*sītāṁ viśāla-nayanāṁ cakame kāla-coditaḥ \|\|*  
*gṛhītvā māyayā veśaṁ carantīṁ vijane vane \|*  
*samāhartuṁ matiṁ cakre tāpasaḥ kila bhāvinīm \|\|*  
*vijñāyā sā ca tad-bhāvaṁ smṛtvā dāśarathiṁ patim \|*  
*jagāma śaraṇaṁ vahnim āvasathyaṁ śuci-smitā \|\|*  
*upatasthe mahā-yogaṁ sarva-pāpa-vināśanam \|*  
*kṛtāñjalī rāma-patnī sākṣāt patim ivācyutam \|\|*  
*namasyāmi mahāyogaṁ kṛtāntaṁ gahanaṁ param \|*  
*dāhakaṁ sarva-bhūtānām īśānaṁ kāla-rūpiṇam \|\| ity ādi \|*  
*iti vahniṁ pūjya japtvā rāma-patnī yaśasvinī \|*  
*dhyāyantī manasā tasthau rāmam unmīlitekṣaṇā \|\|*  
*athāvavasthyād bhagavān havya-vāho maheśvaraḥ \|*  
*āvirāsīt sudīptātmā tejasaiva dahann iva \|*  
*sṛṣṭvā māyāmayīṁ sītāṁ sa rāvaṇa-vadhecchayā \|\|*  
*sītām ādāya dharmiṣṭhāṁ pāvako’ntaradhīyata \|*  
*tāṁ dṛṣṭvā tādṛśīṁ sītāṁ rāvaṇo rākṣaseśvaraḥ \|*  
*samādāya yayau laṅkāṁ sāgarāntara-saṁshtitām \|*  
*kṛtvā ca rāvaṇa-vadhaṁ rāmo lakṣaṇa-saṁyutaḥ \|*  
*samādāyābhavat sītāṁ śaṅkākulita-mānasaḥ \|*  
*sā pratyayāya bhūtānāṁ sītā māyāmayī punaḥ \|*  
*viveśa pāvakaṁ dīptaṁ dadāha jvalano’pi tām \|*  
*dagdhvā māyāmayīṁ sītāṁ bhagavān ugra-dīdhitiḥ \|*  
*rāmāyādarśayat sītāṁ pāvako’bhūt sura-priyaḥ \|*  
*pragṛhya bhartuś caraṇau karābhyāṁ sā sumadhyamā \|*  
*cakāra praṇatiṁ bhūmau rāmāya janakātmajā \|\|*  
*dṛṣṭvā hṛṣṭa-manā rāmo vismayākula-locanaḥ \|*  
*nanāma vahniṁ śirasā toṣayāmāsa rāghavaḥ \|\|*  
*uvāca vahne bhagavan kim eṣā vara-varṇinī \|*  
*dagdhvā bhagavatā pūrvaṁ dṛṣṭyā mat-pārśvam āgatā \|\|*  
*tam āha devo lokānāṁ dāhako havya-vāhanaḥ \|*  
*yathā vṛttaṁ dāśarathiṁ bhūtānām eva sannidhau \|\|*

**A chaste woman is equal to Durgā. There is no doubt. No person can defeat her at any time. Sītā, Rāma’s chaste wife, is famous in the three worlds. The wife of Rāma conquered the king of the demons. Instigated by time, Rāvaṇa, lord of the demons, desired wide-eyed, pure Sītā, the wife of Rāma. Taking a disguise as an ascetic, he decided to steal her as she walked in an isolated forest. Understanding this, she remembered Rāma and, smiling, took shelter of the household fire. She worshipped the great person who destroys all sin. With folded hands she said, “I offer respects to mahāyoga, death personified, who is difficult to understand, who destroys everything, who is the lord of all beings, having the form of time.”**

Thus worshipping fire, with unblinking eyes she chanted and remained there meditating on Rāma. The fire god appeared, shining brightly as if burning everything with his power. Desiring to kill Rāvaṇa, he made an imitation Sītā. He took Sītā, fixed in dharma, and disappeared. Seeing a person similar to Sītā, Rāvaṇa, lord of the demons, took her and went to Laṅka situated across the ocean.

Having killed Rāvaṇa, Rāma, along with Lakṣmaṇa, brought Sītā back. His mind was filled with anxiety. Sītā, made of māyā, to give people faith, entered a blazing fire. The fire burned her up. Having burned up the false Sītā, the fire god, shining brightly, showed Rāma the real Sītā. Fire thus became dear to the devatās. Grasping his two feet, Sītā, daughter of Janaka, offered her respects to Rāma. Overjoyed Rāma, with wide eyes, seeing this, offered respects to the fire with his head and satisfied him. He said, “O fire! O great one! This woman, having been burned by you, has by good fortune come to my side.” (Kūrma Purāṇa 32.513-530)

One should see also Agni Purāṇa. Though this is not made clear by Vālmīki, his not mentioning it serves to nourish karuṇa-rasa. The arrangement for explanations sometimes depends on someone else.

Chaste women have power and more particularly, the Lord’s women have power.

*ya etasmin mahā-bhāge prītiṁ kurvanti mānavāḥ*  
*nārayo ’bhibhavanty etān viṣṇu-pakṣān ivāsurāḥ*

**Enemies cannot harm the devotees who show affection for this greatly fortunate child, just as the demons cannot harm the devatās. (SB** [**10.26.21**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/26.21)**)**

This is Garga’s general statement. This māyā who serves Kṛṣṇa can produce any type of protection, like the household fire of Rāma (which protected Sītā). The spiritual forms were concealed when getting married and sleeping with their apparent husbands. This happened at other times in relation to others also. This was suitable for māyā since her only goal was to increase longing and protect their morality.

The spiritual forms (*svarūpa-siddha*) were present when they spoke to Yaśodā in SB [10.35.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/35.14), when they stopped serving their husbands and children (SB [10.29.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/29.6)) and when they were prevented from joining Kṛṣṇa. (SB [10.29.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/29.8)) Other examples can also be found.

The cause and power that prevented others from harming them is shown:

*ya etasmin mahā-bhāge prītiṁ kurvanti mānavāḥ*  
*nārayo ’bhibhavanty etān viṣṇu-pakṣān ivāsurāḥ*

**Enemies cannot harm the devotees who show affection for this greatly fortunate child, just as the demons cannot harm the devatās. (SB** [**10.8.18**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/8.18)**)**

Though it is said they had children, it refers to the children of the husbands’ relatives. If they had children it would produce rasābhāsa because of irregularity in the vibhāva. It would contradict statements like the following:

*anugrahāya bhaktānāṁ mānuṣaṁ deham āsthitaḥ*  
*bhajate tādṛśīḥ krīḍā yāḥ śrutvā tat-paro bhavet*

**When the Lord assumes a humanlike body to show mercy to his devotees, he engages in such pastimes. Hearing about those pastimes a person becomes dedicated to him. (SB** [**10.33.36**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/33.36)**)**

*evaṁ śaśāṅkāṁśu-virājitā niśāḥ*  
*sa satya-kāmo ’nuratābalā-gaṇaḥ*  
*siṣeva ātmany avaruddha-saurataḥ*  
*sarvāḥ śarat-kāvya-kathā-rasāśrayāḥ*

**With gopīs firmly attached to him with anurāga, Kṛṣṇa, whose desires are always pure, who had fixed in his mind enjoyment with them, served the nights lit by the rays of the moon-- all the nights which give shelter to poetic topics or rasa in autumn. (SB** [**10.33.25**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/33.25)**)**

Feeding babies means they fed milk to others’ children, not that they breast fed their own children.

*mātaraḥ pitaraḥ putrā bhrātaraḥ patayaś ca vaḥ*  
*vicinvanti hy apaśyanto mā kṛḍhvaṁ bandhu-sādhvasam*

**Not finding you at home, your mothers, fathers, sons, brothers and husbands are certainly searching for you. Don’t cause anxiety for your family members. (SB** [**10.29.20**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/29.20)**)**

This statement by Kṛṣṇa is a joke (mentioning sons) to increase rasa. If it were true that they had sons, it would spoil rasa, for he would not accept them as his own in that condition (being contaminated by another man). Sometimes the gopīs address these men as their husbands *(pati*), but this is usage for persons of material vision, not internal vision. For instance they say:

*yat paty-apatya-suhṛdām anuvṛttir aṅga*  
*strīṇāṁ sva-dharma iti dharma-vidā tvayoktam*

**Our dear Kṛṣṇa, as an expert in religion you have advised us that the proper religious duty for women is to faithfully serve their husbands, children and other relatives. (SB** [**10.29.32**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/29.32)**)**

Actually Kṛṣṇa appeared in their minds at all times:

*mām eva dayitaṁ preṣṭham ātmānaṁ manasā gatāḥ*

**Internally, I alone am their dear beloved and, indeed, their very self. (SB** [**10.46.4**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/46.4)**)**

The gopīs also say that they are dedicated to him alone.

*paraṁ saukhyaṁ hi nairāśyaṁ svairiṇy apy āha piṅgalā*  
*taj jānatīnāṁ naḥ kṛṣṇe tathāpy āśā duratyayā*

**The greatest happiness is to renounce all desires, as even the prostitute Piṅgalā has declared. Yet even though we know this, we cannot give up our hopes of attaining Kṛṣṇa. (SB** [**10.47.47**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/47.47)**)**

They say (treating him as their husband):

*gopyaḥ kim ācarad ayaṁ kuśalaṁ sma veṇur*  
*dāmodarādhara-sudhām api gopikānām*  
*bhuṅkte svayaṁ yad avaśiṣṭa-rasaṁ hradinyo*  
*hṛṣyat-tvaco ’śru mumucus taravo yathāryaḥ*

**My dear gopīs! What auspicious activities must the flute have performed to enjoy the nectar of Dāmodara’s lips independently and leave only a taste for us gopīs, for whom that nectar is actually meant! The rivers have their hairs standing on end and its relatives the trees shed tears of pleasure just as forefathers shed tears of joy on seeing the good fortune of their descendants. (SB** [**10.21.9**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/21.9)**)**

*api bata madhu-puryām ārya-putro ’dhunāste*  
*smarati sa pitṛ-gehān saumya bandhūṁś ca gopān*  
*kvacid api sa kathā naḥ kiṅkarīṇāṁ gṛṇīte*  
*bhujam aguru-sugandhaṁ mūrdhny adhāsyat kadā nu*

**O Uddhava, does Kṛṣṇa now reside in Mathurā? Does he remember his father’s houses, his relatives and the cowherd boys? O great soul! Does he ever talk about us, his maidservants? When will he lay on our heads his aguru-scented hand? (SB** [**10.47.21**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/47.21)**)**

And in the five chapters on the rāsa dance it is said that the husbands never hated Kṛṣṇa (SB [10.33.37](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/33.37))

Durvāsā said to them, *sa vo hi svāmī*: he is your husband. (Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad [2.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/2.22)) It is also said *kṛṣṇa-vadhva*: the wives of Kṛṣṇa. (SB [10.33.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/33.7))

*bhagavān api tā rātṛīḥ śāradotphulla-mallikāḥ*  
*vīkṣya rantuṁ manaś cakre yoga-māyām upāśritaḥ*

**Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord, upon seeing those autumn nights scented with blossoming jasmine flowers, taking shelter of his yogamāya, decided to fulfill the desires of the gopīs. (SB** [**10.29.1**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/29.1)**)**

From this it may be concluded that Kṛṣṇa engaged yogamāyā to use or not use her powers and this was known only to her. Thus there were two forms of gopīs which were undetected by the gopīs themselves and others. These two forms were later revealed to the people of Gokula, becoming obvious or revealed by messages from sages and from the sky, in order to produce in the end great happiness without obstacles on the tree of abundant rasa nourished by her for protecting their chastity. These forms were produced in order to avoid hatred from the apparent husbands. *Svarūpeṇa mām eva ramaṇaṁ prāptāḥ*: they attained me as their true husband. Thus Kṛṣṇa alone was their husband. And the desires of the mothers and fathers (that they marry Kṛṣṇa) were also fulfilled.

If Kṛṣṇa actually had illicit relationships, the elder cowherds’ vātsalya for him would disappear. That would not appear if they were married. Bharata however says:

*bahu vāryate khalu yatra pracchanna-kāmukatavaṁ ca*  
*yā ca mitho durlabhatā sā manmathasya paramā ratiḥ*

**That love which causes great opposition because of being against normal dharma, which necessitates the love being kept secret, and which makes it hard for the lovers to meet is the highest type of love. (Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 1.20)**

Rasa will not occur if there is no opposition. Obstruction occurs not by instilling fear, since Kṛṣṇa was most skillful, but rather by giving him shame. Shame for young cultured boys occurs when others infer that he has some secret affair with someone’s wife.

*yatra hrīḥ śrīḥ sthitā tatra yatra śrīs tatra sannatiḥ \|*  
*sannatir hrīs tathā śrīś ca nityaṁ kṛṣṇe mahātmani \|\|*

**Lakṣmī resides where shyness resides. Where Lakṣmī resides, gentle conduct resides. These three are eternally in Kṛṣṇa. (Hari-vaṁśa 2.101.73)**

Kṛṣṇa, who manifests high rank and fresh youth in Vraja, and is the abode of the highest shame, manifests this shame to a high degree. This produces obstacles, secrecy and difficulty in meeting. Shame is of two types: shyness for proper activities and humiliation from improper acts. In the first type one covers one’s inner feelings. This is not too contrary. The second type produces double contrariness if one who is attached to his good name is suspected of vile acts.

Though he did play constantly with the gopīs (*gopa-nārībhir aniśaṁ krīḍayāmāsa keśavaḥ* Padma Purāṇa 6.252.26), it was impossible that Kṛṣṇa played constantly with others’ wives in an illicit way. The countless gopīs were in great grief if they could not attain Kṛṣṇa as their husband though he was naturally so. The elders advised obligatory dharma as the consolation, because of the false appearance (of their being others’ husbands.)

It was said:

*ramya-keli-sukhenaiva gopa-veśa-dharaḥ prabhuḥ \|*  
*bahu-prema-rasenātra māsa-dvayam uvāsa ha \|\|*

**Kṛṣṇa in cowherd dress stayed for two months in Gokula, happily playing and experiencing the rasa of prema.**

After that, it is proper that he gave profuse rasa of prema of a most attractive nature to them. (He married them.) The words *gopa-nārībhiḥ* cannot mean others cowherds’ wives. Similarly in the Third Canto Devahūtī is described as *nṛpa-vadhū*, daughter of the king, rather than wife of the king. Obstacles exist not only in illicit love according to Bharata Muni. Even in married life obstacles exist in the form of cowives as in the case of Ratnāvalī and Yayati. However it is said:

*neṣṭā yad aṅgini rase kavibhir paroḍhā*  
*tad gokulāmbujadṛśāṁ kulam antarena \|*  
*āśāṁsayā rasa-vidher avatāritānāṁ*  
*kaṁsāriṇā rasika-maṇḍala-śekhareṇa \|\|*

**Poets’ objection to married women enjoying with another man as the main rasa applies only to women other than the women of Gokula, whom Kṛṣṇa, the topmost among tasters, made appear in this world because of a special desire for madhura-rasa. (Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 5.3)**

Those who desired Kṛṣṇa as a husband attained him as explained in SB [11.12.13](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.13) (*mat-kāmā ramaṇam)*.

The Lord also fulfilled the vows of the young girls who prayed to have Kṛṣṇa as their husband (*nanda-gopa-sutaṁ devi patiṁ me kuru te namaḥ)*. He accepted them.

*yātābalā vrajaṁ siddhā mayemā raṁsyathā kṣapāḥ*  
*yad uddiśya vratam idaṁ cerur āryārcanaṁ satīḥ*

**Go now, girls, and return to Vraja. Your desire is fulfilled, for in my company you will enjoy the coming nights. After all, this was the purpose of your vow to worship goddess Kātyāyanī, O pure-hearted ones. (SB** [**10.22.27**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/22.27)**)**

He was only like an illicit lover (*jāram*) since he was their husband in disguise. Durvāsā has also said:

*anma-jarābhyāṁ bhinnaḥ sthāṇur ayam acchedyo’yaṁ yo’sau saurye*  
*tiṣṭhati yo’sau goṣu tiṣṭhati yo’sau gāḥ pālayati \| yo’sau gopeṣu tiṣṭhati \|*  
*yo’sau sarveṣu vedeṣu tiṣṭhati \| yo’sau sarva-vedair gīyate \|*  
*yo’sau sarveṣu bhūteṣv āviśya bhūtāni vidadhāti sa vo hi svāmī bhavati*

**He who is beyond birth and old age, who is steady in form, qualities, pastimes and abode, who is indivisible, who resides on the bank of the Yamuna, who lives among the cows, who protects the cows, who lives among the cowherds, who resides in all the Vedas, who is praised in all the Vedas, and who, entering all beings, makes them special, is none other than the husband of you gopīs. (Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad** [**2.21**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/2.21)**)**

### Text 14-15

*tasmāt tvam uddhavotsṛjya codanāṁ praticodanām*  
*pravṛttiṁ ca nivṛttiṁ ca śrotavyaṁ śrutam eva ca*  
*mām ekam eva śaraṇam ātmānaṁ sarva-dehinām*  
*yāhi sarvātma-bhāvena mayā syā hy akuto-bhayaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**O Uddhava! Give up duties and forbidden acts, the path of enjoyment and the path of renunciation, desire to hear more about** ***dharma,*** **and what you have already heard about** ***dharma.*** **Surrender to me alone, the soul of all beings, using all methods, with** ***bhāva*** **in your mind. You will be fearless by my mercy.**

**Комментарий:**

Having narrated that the dearest gopīs attained him, he concludes, including Uddhava in that attainment in two verses. Surrender to me alone, the supreme cherished goal, being the supreme shelter of great devotees, a great goal that can be attained just by association with Nārada, Aṅgirā and others who became my dear associates. Worship me with surrender as the ultimate goal. By my mercy you will be without fear for certain (hi). He shows that this surrender is beneficial to all by its nature. I am the Paramātmā of all beings. Full surrender is explained: do it with complete bhava, while giving up other things. Do not take shelter of jñāna (*śrotavyam śrutam*). The word *eva* indicates that even at other times such things should be given up.

### Text 16

*śrī-uddhava uvāca*  
*saṁśayaḥ śṛṇvato vaṁśa tava yogeśvareśvara*  
*na nivartata ātma-stho yena bhrāmyati me manaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Uddhava said: O Lord of all masters of mystic power, I have heard your words, but the doubt in my heart does not go away. Thus my mind is bewildered.**

**Комментарий:**

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 178:**

Revealing prakaṭa pastimes and entering aprakaṭa pastimes is suggested in questions and answers. Though hearing and understanding your words, I still have doubts. I cannot understand what you said in the last three chapters. Why? This is because your statements such as SB [11.12.10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.10)bewilder my mind. Thinking of what happened to the gopīs’ association with you, I cannot maintain my composure.

Previously he spoke of karma-ṁiśra-bhakti (*mayoditeṣu* [SB 11.10.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/10.1)). All karmas should be given up (*nivṛttam karma seveta,* SB [11.10.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/10.4)). he described in detail ātma-tattva to obtina jñān for devotees with a mixture of jñāna. When Uddhava asked about the jīva’s bondage (SB [11.10.35](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/10.35)) Kṛṣṇa discussed the bound and liberated jīvas, giving the final conclusion. Kṛṣṇa then explained that jñāna cannot be perfected without bhakti (SB [11.11.18](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/11.18)) and then showed pure bhakti (SB [11.11.23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/11.23)). He showed that association with devotee is the cause of bhakti. Uddhava asked about the best bhakti and the best devotee (SB [11.11.23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/11.23)) and the Lord explained about them and confirmed the power of pure association. (SB [11.11.48](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/11.48)) He advised Uddhava to give karma and jñāna completely. (SB [11.12.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.14))

Uddhava now speaks so that there will be no doubts. Having heard your words and understood them I have some doubt. I am unable to understand the mahāvākyas in the last three chapters. The doubt does not go away. Because of that my mind is confused. I am very perplexed. Therefore again teach thoroughly. I have been unable to grasp the final conclusion of all the topic. I have heard imperfectly, though I heard.

### Text 17

*śrī-bhagavān uvāca*  
*sa eṣa jīvo vivara-prasūtiḥ*  
*prāṇena ghoṣeṇa guhāṁ praviṣṭaḥ*  
*mano-mayaṁ sūkṣmam upetya rūpaṁ*  
*mātrā svaro varṇa iti sthaviṣṭhaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord said: I am the Supreme Lord who gives life to every living being. Manifesting in the various** ***cakras*****, I enter the** ***mūlādhāra-cakra*** **of Brahmā along with** ***prāṇa*** **phase of subtle** ***parā*** **sound. I then rise to the** ***maṇipūra-cakra*** **in the mental phase as** ***paśyantī*** **and to (the** ***anāhata–cakra,*** **the intellectual phase) in subtle form as** ***madhyamā*****. I then take the gross form of short and longs sounds, different intonations, and the syllables of the alphabet.**

**Комментарий:**

There are two types of anxiety (for himself and the gopīs). Kṛṣṇa said that he left the gopīs (SB [11.12.10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.10)). Based on that Uddhava feared Kṛṣṇa would also leave him. Did he meet with them somewhere, somehow? Uddhava was anxious to know the details. To remove the two types of anxiety, the present verse is spoken. It destroys the anxiety that he would be left alone, being unable to accept the meaning of the description given by Kṛṣṇa, by the first meaning of the statement.

He describes how he manifests himself as the sound of all the Vedas in order to show that he is the shelter, the leader, and the meaning of all the Vedas, as expressed in SB [11.12.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/12.14).

The description of the four main cakras mentioned are in relation to the manifestation of the Vedas from Brahmā. This is correlated with the description in [11.21.38-40](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/21.38):

*yathorṇanābhir hṛdayād ūrṇām udvamate mukhāt*  
*ākāśād ghoṣavān prāṇo manasā sparśa-rūpiṇā*  
*chando-mayo ’mṛta-mayaḥ sahasra-padavīṁ prabhuḥ*  
*oṁkārād vyañjita-sparśa- svaroṣmāntastha-bhūṣitām*  
*vicitra-bhāṣā-vitatāṁ chandobhiś catur-uttaraiḥ*  
*ananta-pārāṁ bṛhatīṁ sṛjaty ākṣipate svayam*

**Just as a spider brings forth from its heart its web and emits it through its mouth, the Supreme Lord, made of supreme bliss, made of the Vedas, taking support of ether, appears as** ***parā*** **sound made of** ***prāṇa,*** **and then creates and destroys, by his mind which produces the consonants and the manifest Vedas--which are unlimited in sound and meaning, decorated with the consonants, vowels, sibilants, and semivowels emanating from the subtle sound** ***oṁ,*** **and which are filled with a variety of languages and meters, each four syllables longer than the previous.**

*Prāṇa* refers to the upādhi in the form of Hiraṇyagarbha, whose material substance is *nāda* (*ghoṣavān*). The Vedas are called unlimited (*ananta-pārām*). They are not the samāṣti (sum of jīvas), since they are not restricted by time or space of the prāṇa and other factors. Śrīdhara Svāmī quotes two verses from śruti:

*catvari-vak-parimitā padāni tāni vidur brāhmaṇā ye manīṣiṇaḥ*  
*guha trīṇi nihitā neṅgayanti turīyaṁ vāco manuṣyā vadanti*

**Brāhmaṇas who are wise know that there are four stages of evolution of sound. Three are hidden, and do not reveal themselves. Men call the fourth stage “words.”**

*yā sā mitrāvaruṇa sadanād uccarantī triśaṣṭiṁ*  
*varṇān antaḥ prakaṭa-karaṇaiḥ prāṇa-saṁgāt prasute*  
*tāṁ paśyaṇtīm prathamam uditāṁ madhyamāṁ buddhī-saṁsthāṁ*  
*vācaṁ vaktre karaṇa-viśadāṁ vaikharīṁ ca prapadye*

**Parā manifests from the ādhāra-cakra of Agni and Soma with prāṇa in a subtle form. It produces the sixty-three varṇas of the alphabet by means of two internal senses and a manifested sense internally. The first manifestation is called paśyāntī (that which is not uttered). The third cakra, endowed with discrimination, and which understands “I am uttering this” is called madhyamā. and the fourth producing purity is called vaikhārī.**

There are three vowels with short, long and very long pronunciation, making 9, plus short and long *ṛ* and *l*. making 13. The dipthongs (*e ai o au*) can be long or very long, making 8 more. Total is 21. High, medium and low pitches are included in this total. Visarga, anusvara, jihvāmūlīya and upadhmānīya bring the total to 25.

There are 25 consonants. That total is then 50. Ya, va and la either nasalized or unnasalized – 6 and *r* makes 57. (Three sibilants and *h* make 60).

By the second meaning, his second anxiety is destroyed.

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 179:**

The Lord speaks to remove his doubt in two verses and relieves his heart.

That person *(saḥ*), meaning me, the cause of life (*jīvaḥ*), giving life to Vraja-- or the Supreme Lord--manifested in prakaṭa pastimes (*prasūtiḥ*) from the aprakaṭa pastimes (*vivarta*) along with Vraja (*ghoṣeṇa*), equal to my very life (*prāṇeṇa),* and then entered aprakaṭa pastimes (*guḥam praviṣṭaḥ*). I manifest (*sthaviṣṭaḥ*) myself to my intimate associates with my senses (*mātrā*), words (*svara*), song *(varṇa)* and form. To others I am invisible (*sūkṣam*). For more distant devotees I am understood by the mind (*manomayam*).

### Text 18

*yathānalaḥ khe ’nila-bandhur uṣmā*  
*balena dāruṇy adhimathyamānaḥ*  
*aṇuḥ prajāto haviṣā samedhate*  
*tathaiva me vyaktir iyaṁ hi vāṇī*

**Перевод:**

**When sticks of kindling wood are vigorously rubbed together, heat is first situated as heat in the wood, and then by assistance of air, a spark of fire appears. Once the fire is kindled, ghee is added and the fire blazes. Similarly, I gradually become manifest as the sound of the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 180:**

The example of appearance of the Lord is partial, showing the gradual stages. In the Third Canto Uddhava also says *ajo’pi jāto bhagavān yathāgniḥ*: the Lord though unborn, appears like fire. (SB [3.2.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.15)) *Vyaktiḥ* means manifestation. I manifest in this way because (*hi)* this is my word, and I alone know my secret. It is not impossible to do.

### Text 19

*evaṁ gadiḥ karma gatir visargo*  
*ghrāṇo raso dṛk sparśaḥ śrutiś ca*  
*saṅkalpa-vijñānam athābhimānaḥ*  
*sūtraṁ rajaḥ-sattva-tamo-vikāraḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, speech, action, motion, excretion, smelling, tasting, seeing, touching, hearing, deciding, discerning, identity,** ***mahat-tattva*****, and transformations of** ***rajas, sattva*** **and** ***tamas*** **are my material manifestation.**

**Комментарий:**

Having shown that the Vedas arise from himself, he shows the manifestation of functions material speech etc. in the world, in order that people give up absorption in the world. Just as the functions of Vedic sound arise through para etc., material speech and other functions arise, having a different meaning as in the two previous verses. But the Vedic sound arises by the svarūpa-śakti whereas material sound arises by māyā. And just as speech arises so does karma etc.

### Text 20

*ayaṁ hi jīvas tri-vṛd abja-yonir*  
*avyakta eko vayasā sa ādyaḥ*  
*viśliṣṭa-śaktir bahudheva bhāti*  
*bījāni yoniṁ pratipadya yadvat*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord becomes the Lord made of the three** ***guṇas.*** **He is one entity and invisible, but by time, possessing many energies, he becomes many forms, just as one seed among many seeds sprouts.**

**Комментарий:**

An example is given in the second half of the verse. The Lord becomes many just as one seed among many sprouts.

### Text 21

*yasminn idaṁ protam aśeṣam otaṁ*  
*paṭo yathā tantu-vitāna-saṁsthaḥ*  
*ya eṣa saṁsāra-taruḥ purāṇaḥ*  
*karmātmakaḥ puṣpa-phale prasūte*

**Перевод:**

**This universe exists in the Lord like a cloth with vertical and horizontal threads. It is like a tree, with no beginning, consisting of the force of** ***māyā,*** **giving birth to the flowers and fruits of happiness and distress.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to understand more clearly he gives metaphors to describe the world.

### Text 23

*adanti caikaṁ phalam asya gṛdhrā*  
*grāme-carā ekam araṇya-vāsāḥ*  
*haṁsā ya ekaṁ bahu-rūpam ijyair*  
*māyā-mayaṁ veda sa veda vedam*

**Перевод:**

**The vultures or householders eat one fruit of the tree and the swans or** ***sannyāsīs*** **eat another fruit of the tree. He who knows me as the universe produced by** ***māyā*** **and as many spiritual forms knows the meaning of the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

After explaining the Vedas and the material universe, he shows in two verses by combining the two together, the conclusion of what was previously stated in verse 14. There are different qualifications for practice and result in what was stated in the Vedas. The swans or the sannyāsīs (*araṇya-vāsāḥ*), who determine the essence from the non-essence, enjoy happiness composed of knowledge and as a result go to Brahman or attain liberation in Brahman. There is no falling into suffering at all from things connected with knowledge. He who knows me to be the form of the universe created by māyā-śakti, and to be of many forms such as the caturvyūha, by the variety of the svarūpa-śakti, knows the essence of the Vedas.

### Text 24

*evaṁ gurūpāsanayaika-bhaktyā*  
*vidyā-kuṭhāreṇa śitena dhīraḥ*  
*vivṛścya jīvāśayam apramattaḥ*  
*sampadya cātmānam atha tyajāstram*

**Перевод:**

**Thus, by worshipping** ***guru,*** **by the axe of** ***jñāna*** **made sharp by** ***bhakti*** **even in a secondary role, by being steady, destroy the subtle body, and with attention attain Paramātmā. Then give up the weapon of** ***jñāna.***

**Комментарий:**

The result of knowledge is described. In the manner described (*evam*), by worshipping guru, by the axe of knowledge, generated from material antaḥkaraṇa, sharpened by prominence of bhakti, purify external portions of the antaḥkaraṇa and then give up the weapon which destroys saṁsāra, knowledge generated by the material antaḥkaraṇa. After giving up that knowledge, attaining Paramātmā (*ātmānam*) by one’s own knowledge, becoming liberated, experience the happiness as suitable to that condition. It will be said:

*evaṁ vimṛśya guṇato manasas try-avasthā*  
*man-māyayā mayi kṛtā iti niścitārthāḥ*  
*sañchidya hārdam anumāna-sad-ukti-tīkṣṇa*  
*jñānāsinā bhajata mākhila-saṁśayādhim*

**Considering that the three states of the intelligence arising from the** ***guṇas*** **are created by my** ***avidyā*** **within me, being firmly convinced of your nature as** ***ātmā,*** **and cutting the three states by the sharp sword of knowledge, by inference and by scriptural statements, worship me, the destroyer of all doubts. (SB** [**11.13.33**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/13.33)**)**

## Chapter Thirteen

### Text 2

*sattvād dharmo bhaved vṛddhāt puṁso mad-bhakti-lakṣaṇaḥ*  
*sāttvikopāsayā sattva tato dharmaḥ pravartate*

**Перевод:**

**From an increase in** ***sattva,*** ***dharma*****, characterized by secondary** ***bhakti*** **to me, becomes prominent. One can strengthen** ***sattva*** **by cultivation of** ***sattvika*** **items. From that** ***sattva, dharma*** **arises.**

**Комментарий:**

Having attained association with devotees as was previously mentioned, dharma characterized by bhakti increases by sattva. It is like a special campaka seed planted in the form of a garland. By sprinkling with milk it grows with strength.

### Text 3

*dharmo rajas tamo hanyāt sattva-vṛddhir anuttamaḥ*  
*āśu naśyati tan-mūlo hy adharma ubhaye hate*

**Перевод:**

***Dharma,*** **strengthened by** ***sattva,*** **destroys the influence of** ***rajas*** **and** ***tamas.*** **When** ***rajas*** **and** ***tamas*** **are destroyed,** ***adharma,*** **caused by them, is quickly vanquished.**

**Комментарий:**

Sattva and dharma mutually increase each other.

### Text 5

*tat tat sāttvikam evaiṣāṁ yad yad vṛddhāḥ pracakṣate*  
*nindanti tāmasaṁ tat tad rājasaṁ tad-upekṣitam*

**Перевод:**

**Among the ten items, the great sages have praised and recommended those that are in** ***sattva.*** **They have rejected those in** ***tamas*** **and show indifference for those in** ***rajas.***

**Комментарий:**

The sages are persons like Vyāsa. The good and bad aspects of sattva and other guṇas are shown in Padma Purāṇa.

### Text 6

*sāttvikāny eva seveta pumān sattva-vivṛddhaye*  
*tato dharmas tato jñāna yāvat smṛtir apohanam*

**Перевод:**

**Until one realizes** ***ātmā*** **and destroys the** ***guṇas*** **and the gross and subtle bodies, one must use** ***sattvika*** **items to increase** ***sattva,*** **which increases** ***dharma*** **and then gives rise to** ***jñāna.***

**Комментарий:**

By cultivating sattva, jñāna manifests. One should serve items of sattva to develop realization of ātmā.

### Text 8

*śrī-uddhava uvāca*  
*vidanti martyāḥ prāyeṇa viṣayān padam āpadām*  
*tathāpi bhuñjate kṛṣṇa tat kathaṁ śva-kharāja-vat*

**Перевод:**

**Uddhava said: O Kṛṣṇa! Generally human beings know that material life brings great future unhappiness, and still they try to enjoy material life. How can a person in knowledge enjoy just like a dog, an ass or a goat?**

**Комментарий:**

Though they know, why do they enjoy like dogs who do not know? Those who do not know are the same as dogs and enjoy. They know that material objects are the fixed source (*padam*) of future suffering (*āpadam*). This is described in verse 11.

The dog after enjoying sex becomes devoid of a relationship. The donkey, after enjoying a handful of grass shown by the washer man, carries a heavy load. The goat, after enjoying various things constantly, has his head cut off. These animals do not know these results.

### Text 9-10

*śrī-bhagavān uvāca*  
*aham ity anyathā-buddhiḥ pramattasya yathā hṛdi*  
*utsarpati rajo ghoraṁ tato vaikārikaṁ manaḥ*  
*rajo-yuktasya manasaḥ saṅkalpaḥ sa-vikalpakaḥ*  
*tataḥ kāmo guṇa-dhyānād duḥsahaḥ syād dhi durmateḥ*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord said: When the inattentive person mistakenly thinks in his mind that he is the body, terrible** ***rajas*** **overcomes the sattvika mind. The mind in** ***rajas*** **then decides that certain objects are enjoyable and concentrates on them. Then uncontrollable desire arises in the foolish person from absorbing himself in the qualities of the desired object.**

**Комментарий:**

The person loses discriminatory power by particular karmas. When he thinks that the body is the ātmā (*anyathā buddhiḥ*) and that thinking enters the heart, rajas enters and overcomes the sattvika mind. The mind becomes difficult to control. The result is then described.

### Text 11

*karoti kāma-vaśa-gaḥ karmāṇy avijitendriyaḥ*  
*duḥkhodarkāṇi sampaśyan rajo-vega-vimohitaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**One who does not control the material senses comes under the control of material desires, and, bewildered by the strong push of** ***rajas,*** **performs material activities, although clearly understanding that the result will be future unhappiness.**

**Комментарий:**

Though he knows (*saṁpaśyan*) that the result will be suffering he performs the actions.

### Text 14

*etāvān yoga ādiṣṭo mac-chiṣyaiḥ sanakādibhiḥ*  
*sarvato mana ākṛṣya mayy addhāveśyate yathā*

**Перевод:**

**This** ***yoga*** **system was taught by my devotees, headed by Sanaka. Having withdrawn the mind from all other objects, one should directly and appropriately absorb it in me.**

**Комментарий:**

*Addhā* means directly.

### Text 15

*śrī-uddhava uvāca*  
*yadā tvaṁ sanakādibhyo yena rūpeṇa keśava*  
*yogam ādiṣṭavān etad rūpam icchāmi veditum*

**Перевод:**

**Uddhava said: O Keśava! I desire to know when and in what form did you instruct the science of** ***yoga*** **to Sanaka and his brothers.**

**Комментарий:**

I desire to know the teachings (*rūpam*) when you, the form of parabrahman in human form, the avatārī, taking the form of an avatāra (*yena rūpeṇa*), gave instructions.

### Text 18

*śrī-bhagavān uvāca*  
*evaṁ pṛṣṭo mahā-devaḥ svayambhūr bhūta-bhāvanaḥ*  
*dhyāyamānaḥ praśna-bījaṁ nābhyapadyata karma-dhīḥ*

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord said: On being asked, Brahmā, who was born directly from the body of the Lord, who is the creator of all living entities within the material world, and who was the best of the** ***devatās,*** **could not understand the nature of** ***ātmā*** **after considering the matter because his mind was absorbed in creation of the universe.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord gave Brahmā benedictions:

*yāvān ahaṁ yathā-bhāvo yad-rūpa-guṇa-karmakaḥ \|*  
*tathaiva tattva-vijñānam astu te mad-anugrahāt \|\|*

**By my mercy, may you attain perfect realization of whatever dimensions, intentions, forms, qualities and pastimes I manifest. (SB** [**2.9.32**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/9.32)**)**

*etan mataṁ samātiṣṭha parameṇa samādhinā \|*  
*bhavān kalpa-vikalpeṣu na vimuhyati karhicit \|\|*

**Follow my instructions in detail, with extreme concentration. You will not be bewildered at any time during your lifetime and its subdivisions. (SB** [**2.9.37**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/9.37)**)**

*ṛṣim ādyaṁ na badhnāti pāpīyāṁs tvāṁ rajo-guṇaḥ*  
*yan mano mayi nirbaddhaṁ prajāḥ saṁsṛjato ’pi te*

**Since your mind will be absorbed in me, though you will create the offspring, you, a great sage, will not be bound by** ***rajoguṇa.*** **(SB** [**3.9.35**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/9.35)**)**
