Their intelligence was covered by the will of the Lord (*diṣṭa*). All this has been done by māyā, as explained in the next verse. This will be established. Contrary stories told in other Purāṇas are also explanations created by māyā.

### Text 24

*brahma-śāpopasṛṣṭānāṁ kṛṣṇa-māyāvṛtātmanām*  
*spardhā-krodhaḥ kṣayaṁ ninye vaiṇavo ’gnir yathāvanam*

**Перевод:**

**The violent anger of these warriors, who were overcome by the** ***brāhmaṇas’*** **curse and bewildered by Kṛṣṇa’s illusory potency, now led them to their annihilation, just as a fire that starts in a bamboo grove destroys even places beyond the forest.**

**Комментарий:**

The anger led to their destruction (*kṣayam*). The actual meaning is that the destruction led them to the eternal abode. The fire in the bamboo grove spread accidently to human habitation (a*vanam*).

### Text 25

*evaṁ naṣṭeṣu sarveṣu kuleṣu sveṣu keśavaḥ*  
*avatārito bhuvo bhāra iti mene ’vaśeṣitaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**When all the members of his dynasty were thus destroyed, Kṛṣṇa thought to himself that the earth would suffer.**

**Комментарий:**

He thought that on disappearing and giving up the earth, the earth would suffer great difficulties.

### Text 26

*rāmaḥ samudra-velāyāṁ yogam āsthāya pauruṣam*  
*tatyāja lokaṁ mānuṣyaṁ saṁyojyātmānam ātmani*

**Перевод:**

**Balarāma then sat down on the shore of the ocean and ascertained constant union with Kṛṣṇa. Merging his manifest form with his unmanifest form, he left this earth planet.**

**Комментарий:**

Ascertaining constant union with Kṛṣṇa (*pauruṣam*), uniting himself in his manifestation of the prakaṭa pastimes with the manifestation of the aprakaṭa pastimes (*ātmani*), considering them non-different, Balarāma left the earth.

### Text 27

*rāma-niryāṇam ālokya bhagavān devakī-sutaḥ*  
*niṣasāda dharopasthe tuṣṇīm āsādya pippalam*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa, the son of Devakī, seeing the departure of Balarāma, silently sat down on the ground under a nearby** ***pippala*** **tree.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa’s actions are described in six verses.

### Text 40

*ity ādiṣṭo bhagavatā kṛṣṇenecchā-śarīriṇā*  
*triḥ parikramya taṁ natvā vimānena divaṁ yayau*

**Перевод:**

**So instructed by the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa, who assumes the best body by his own will, the hunter circumambulated the Lord three times and bowed down to him. Then the hunter departed in an airplane to the spiritual sky.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord manifests his form in the world by his will (*icchā-śarīriṇā*). There is no other cause.

### Text 44

*iti bruvati sūte vai ratho garuḍa-lāñchanaḥ*  
*kham utpapāta rājendra sāśva-dhvaja udīkṣataḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Śukadeva Gosvāmī continued: O foremost of kings! While the chariot driver was still speaking, before his very eyes the Lord’s chariot rose up into the sky along with its horses and its flag, which was marked with the emblem of Garuḍa.**

**Комментарий:**

It is said:

*evaṁ dhyāyati govinda ākāśāt sūrya-varcasau*  
*rathāv upasthitau sadyaḥ sa-sūtau sa-paricchadau*

**As Kṛṣṇa, protector of the earth, was thinking in this way, two chariots as effulgent as the sun suddenly descended from the sky. They were complete with drivers and equipment. (SB** [**10.50.11**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/50.11)**)**

Now a chariot appeared similar to Dāruka’s. He was astonished. Dāruka was left there to carry out actions at a later time.

### Text 46

*gaccha dvāravatīṁ sūta jñātīnāṁ nidhanaṁ mithaḥ*  
*saṅkarṣaṇasya niryāṇaṁ bandhubhyo brūhi mad-daśām*

**Перевод:**

**O driver, go to Dvārakā and tell our family members about the mutual destruction of their relatives, the disappearance of Saṅkarṣaṇa and my condition of unmanifest pastimes.**

**Комментарий:**

Tell them of the destruction (*nidhanam*) of their relatives. The real meaning is “Tell them of the attainment of complete wealth of unmanifested pastimes.” They had followed the Lord.

### Text 47

*dvārakāyāṁ ca na stheyaṁ bhavadbhiś ca sva-bandhubhiḥ*  
*mayā tyaktāṁ yadu-purīṁ samudraḥ plāvayiṣyati*

**Перевод:**

**You and your relatives should not remain in Dvārakā, the capital of the Yadus, because once I have abandoned that city it will be inundated by the ocean.**

**Комментарий:**

The ocean would actually surrounded the place so that they would leave the place to avoid the flooding.

### Text 49

*tvaṁ tu mad-dharmam āsthāya jñāna-niṣṭha upekṣakaḥ*  
*man-māyā-racitām etāṁ vijñayopaśamaṁ vraja*

**Перевод:**

**You, Dāruka, should be firmly situated in devotion to me, fixed in spiritual knowledge and unattached to material considerations. Understanding these pastimes to be a display of my illusory potency, you should remain peaceful.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to pacify Dāruka, he explains that the pastimes of the club up to Arjuna’s defeat are a display of the Lord’s māyā.

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 123:**

You, Dāruka, fixed in knowledge, knowing the truth of my pastimes, trusting *(āsthāya*) my nature of associating with my associates as an equal and of protecting my devotees, knowing that this disappearance pastime revealed at this time to be my illusory work, being indifferent to lamentation rising by external vision, free from agitation of the heart (*upaśamam),* attain me *(vraja).* The word *tu* indicates that others would be bewildered but it is not suitable for Dāruka to be bewildered since Dāruka is eternal, having appeared from Vaikuṇṭha. The word *etām* (this pastime) indicates one should not explain it in a different way since he had just received the meaning of that pastime. The pastime was not what it appeared to be.

One sees this arrangement in the Mahābhārata, where Yamarāja speaks to Yudhiṣṭhira about Bhīma and others going to hell:

*na ca te bhrātaraṁ pārtha! narakasthā viśāṁ pate \|*  
*māyaiṣā devarājena mahendreṇa prayojitā \|\|*

**O king! Your brother is not in hell. This is an illusion created by the Lord.**

### Text 50

*ity uktas taṁ parikramya namaskṛtya punaḥ punaḥ*  
*tat-pādau śīrṣṇy upādhāya durmanāḥ prayayau purīm*

**Перевод:**

**Thus ordered, Dāruka circumambulated the Lord and offered obeisances to him again and again. He placed Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet upon his head and then with a sad heart went back to the city.**

**Комментарий:**

He was sad, according to external appearance.

## Chapter Thirty-one

### Text 5

*bhagavān pitāmahaṁ vīkṣya vibhūtīr ātmano vibhuḥ*  
*saṁyojyātmani cātmānaṁ padma-netre nyamīlayat*

**Перевод:**

**Seeing Brahmā, the grandfather of the universe, along with the other** ***devatās,*** **who were his** ***vibhūtis,*** **the Lord fixed his mind within himself, and closed his eyes.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord, seeing Brahmā and the devatās (*vibhūtīḥ)*, his expansions, drew them near and closed his eyes, fixing his mind on his svarūpa in order to teach how to give up the body, giving up siddhis, and to hide the nature of his form from them.

### Text 6

*lokābhirāmāṁ sva-tanuṁ dhāraṇā-dhyāna-maṅgalam*  
*yoga-dhāraṇayāgneyyā- dagdhvā dhāmāviśat svakam*

**Перевод:**

**Without performing meditation to burn up his body, which was all-attractive to world and the object of all contemplation and meditation, Kṛṣṇa entered into his abode.**

**Комментарий:**

Though there seems to some contrary meaning, the meaning is discerned through the verses containing the instructions, similarly to the meaning of statements in the Upaniṣads. *Ākāśas tal-liṅgād*: the word *ākāśa* denotes Brahman, because of indications in the text. (*Vedānta–sūtra* [1.1.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/1.1.22))

Destroying the idea that his body was burned up, the word can also be taken as *adaghvā* (not being burned up). His body was attractive to the whole world. This indicates that he was the shelter of the universe. Taking a wider meaning of the word *loka,* it includes the eternal associates of Mahā-vaikuṇṭha, the ātmārāmas or jñānīs and living entities down to the plants. He gave pleasure to them all. What more can be said? He gave pleasure to the greatest devotees but he was also the shelter of the sādhakas, because he was the auspicious object of meditation and contemplation (*dhāraṇa-dhyāna-maṅgalam*). For the meditators he was the form of auspiciousness. How could it be otherwise?

His abode and his body were dependent on him (*sva, svakam*). These two adjectives indicate his svarūpa. *Svakam* indicates a secondary item and *sva-tanum* indicates the chief item (to emphasize the nature of his body). The compound indicates a continuous relationship: he always possesses that body. *Dhāma svakam* indicates dependence on the Lord, and thus why would *sva-tanu* also not indicate dependence of his body on the Lord’s will? Thus his body was not burned up through meditation. What purpose would burning up his body serve? It is like scratching a limb which is not itchy.

Śrīdhara Svāmī explains that the yogīs die by their will, by burning up their bodies. But Kṛṣṇa’s departure was different. He meditated on fire. That is true. But his body was not burned up and he entered his abode. After teaching yogīs how to give up the body, he disappeared. It cannot be otherwise. This is one astonishing pastime.

*ity aṅgopadiśanty eke vismṛtya prāg udāhṛtam*  
*muni-vāsa-nivāse kiṁ ghaṭetāriṣṭa-darśanam*

**Some men proposed that the troubles were due to Akrūra’s absence, but they had forgotten the glories of the Supreme Lord, which they themselves had so often described. Indeed, how can calamities occur in a place where the Lord, the residence of all the sages, resides? (SB** [**10.57.31**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/57.31)**)**

*evaṁ vadanti rājarṣe ṛṣayaḥ kecanānvitāḥ*  
*yat sva-vāco virudhyeta nūnaṁ te na smaranty uta*

**Such is the account given by some sages, O wise King, but those who speak in this illogical way are contradicting themselves, having forgotten their own previous statements. (SB** [**10.77.30**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/77.30)**)**

Though this is the Lord’s nature, other sages have different ideas-- which are not corrected by the king of sages Śukadeva. What to speak of this incident. In order to refute the contrary ideas the phrase *lokābhirāmām* is modified by various words.

In order to hide his nature, the Lord shows things by māyā. These appearances are what various sages describe. This will be shown in verse 8. Even previous to this incident, his form was not manifest to some persons. *Nāhaṁ prakāśaḥ sarvasya yoga-māyā-samāvṛtaḥ*: I am not revealed to everyone, but am hidden by yoga-māyā. (BG [9.25](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.25)) They imagine another form as in *virāḍ aviduṣam*: the ignorant saw Kṛṣṇa as material. (SB [10.43.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/43.17)) By the Lord’s māyā he appears differently by eyes made to see illusion They describe the Lord’s body as material. He did not burn up his body but burned a body made by his māyā.

The same is indicated by *icchā-śarīrābhiprāyeṇa*: he accepted a body by his will. The contrary ideas are intolerable. By his free will, he created a body and by his free will he disappeared. Meditating on fire was also an appearance. These terms are explained in Kṛṣṇa Sandarbha.

Or according to the statement that the Lord manifests his form in this world by his desire (*icchā-śarīram*) he performed his actions mentioned in this verse by manifesting a particular body (getting burned up) to fulfill his desires. By his desire, māyā acted in a certain way. That is also a proper explanation. The meaning that the sages show by indirect expression is proper since it follows the Lord’s desire.

*vedā brahmātma-viṣayās tri-kāṇḍa-viṣayā ime*  
*parokṣa-vādā ṛṣayaḥ parokṣaṁ mama ca priyam*

**The Vedas, divided into three divisions, ultimately reveal worship of the Lord. The Vedic sages and** ***mantras*****, however, state this indirectly, since they know that I prefer this indirect method. (SB** [**11.21.35**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/21.35)**)**

### Text 10

*brahma-rudrādayas te tu dṛṣṭvā yoga-gatiṁ hareḥ*  
*vismitās tāṁ praśaṁsantaḥ svaṁ svaṁ lokaṁ yayus tadā*

**Перевод:**

**A few of the** ***devatās,*** **however—notably Brahmā and Śiva—could ascertain that the Lord’s mystic power was working, and thus they became astonished. All the** ***devatās*** **praised the Lord’s mystic power and then returned to their own planets.**

**Комментарий:**

Some of the devatās perceived the truth of the matter.

### Text 11

*rājan parasya tanu-bhṛj-jananāpyayehā*  
*māyā-viḍambanam avehi yathā naṭasya*  
*sṛṣṭvātmanedam anuviśya vihṛtya cānte*  
*saṁhṛtya cātma-mahinoparataḥ sa āste*

**Перевод:**

**O King! You should understand that the Supreme Lord’s appearance and disappearance, which resemble those of embodied conditioned souls, are actually a show enacted by his illusory energy, just like the performance of an actor. After creating this illusion he entered into it, played within it for some time, and at last destroyed it, withdrawing from it by his own power. Thus he remains.**

**Комментарий:**

The Yādavas could not have material bodies, what to speak of Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa.

The siddhānta is given.

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 124:**

Nārada has said concerning his own body:

*prayujyamāne mayi tāṁ śuddhāṁ bhāgavatīṁ tanum \|*  
*ārabdha-karma-nirvāṇo nyapatat pāñca-bhautikaḥ \|\|*

**Having been awarded a transcendental body befitting an associate of the Lord, the body made of five material elements, with karmas relating to the present body, fell away. (SB** [**1.6.28**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/6.28)**)**

Know that the activities *(īhāḥ)* of the associates of Kṛṣṇa (*parasya),* in which he maintain bodies (*tanu-bhṛt*), (ie. the Yadus), which appear and disappear (*jananāpyaya*), are only an imitation produced by the Lord’s māyā. It is similar to a magician (*naṭaḥ)*. The magician is alive, but shows that he is killed, burned and then shows that he creates a new body. It is not surprising since the Lord has inconceivable śakti to create the universe etc. That is explained in the second part of the verse. Thus, for the foolish people, shadow forms of Balarāma and others, are introduced in the description of the illusory pastime to cause a different perception. In Skanda Purāṇa, Lakṣmaṇa’s death should not be accepted. In the Nārāyaṇa kavaca it is shown that Balarāma is acknowledged as the eternal protector of the worshipper, having powers more extraordinary than Śeṣa’s.

Jarāsandha also says:

*tava rāma yadi śraddhā yudhyasva dhairyam udvaha*  
*hitvā vā mac-charaiś chinnaṁ dehaṁ svar yāhi māṁ jahi*

**You, Balarāma, should gather your courage and fight with me, if you think you can do it. You may either give up your body when it is cut to pieces by my arrows, and thus attain to heaven, or else kill me. (SB** [**10.50.18**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/50.18)**)**

Śrīdhara Svāmī gives the correct meaning to this. Thinking that Balarāma’s body was indestructible, out of dissatisfaction, he speaks the alternative: kill me.

*Lokābhirāmāṁ sva-tanuṁ*: his body was most attractive to all people. (SB [11.31.6](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/31.6)) Śrīdhara Svāmī comments on this, saying his body could not be burned, since the Lord has powers superior to the powers of a yogī. The skillful explanations are established by *kaimutya*. He says “Even today worshippers see that beautiful form.” Thus, he agrees with what has been said. The worshippers see the Lord with his associates. It is not possible that they die, because they have spiritual bodies.

### Text 12

*martyena yo guru-sutaṁ yama-loka-nītaṁ*  
*tvāṁ cānayac charaṇa-daḥ paramāstra-dagdham*  
*jigye ’ntakāntakam apīśam asāv anīśaḥ*  
*kiṁ svāvane svar anayan mṛgayuṁ sa-deham*

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa brought the son of his** ***guru*** **back from the planet of death in the boy’s selfsame body, and as the ultimate giver of protection he saved you also when you were burned by the weapon of Aśvatthāmā. He conquered in battle even Śiva, who deals death to the agents of death, and he sent the hunter Jarā directly to Vaikuṇṭha in his human body. How could such a personality be unable to protect himself?**

**Комментарий:**

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 125:**

Since the associates are protected by Kṛṣṇa they cannot be destroyed.

Kṛṣṇa brought the son of his guru who had gone to Yamaloka in the body born of his guru, which had been devoured by Pañcajana. The power of a brahma weapon should not be considered strong: he protected you from the brahmāstra when you were burned by it. What more can be said? He conquered Śiva in a battle of arrows. He sent the hunter Jarā in his body to a special Vaikuṇṭha *(svaḥ*). Can he not protect the Yadus *(sva*)? Thus their destruction is a faulty perception and not according to the actual pastime. He took them to his abode in their spiritual bodies.

### Text 13

*tathāpy aśeṣa-sthiti-sambhavāpyayeṣv*  
*ananya-hetur yad aśeṣa-śakti-dhṛk*  
*naicchat praṇetuṁ vapur atra śeṣitaṁ*  
*martyena kiṁ sva-stha-gatiṁ pradarśayan*

**Перевод:**

**Although Kṛṣṇa, being the possessor of infinite powers, is the only cause of the creation, maintenance and destruction of innumerable planets, he did not desire to remain with his manifested pastimes on earth. What is the use of this mortal world or its people? But he made known his destination to the** ***devatās.***

**Комментарий:**

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 126:**

“Let us accept that the associates went to the Lord’s abode in their bodies. There, Svayam Bhagavān exists. They have no pain of separation. If the Lord is capable of doing this, why does he not have other persons similar to them appear in the material world along with him and stay there for some time to give mercy to mortals along with them?” The Lord and his associates have unshakeable bonds of friendship (and cannot be separated). Though he is the only cause of creation, maintenance and destruction, because *(yat*) he has unlimited powers of this nature, having made the Yadus disappear, he did not desire to keep his body there any longer. He took his body to his abode. The reason is given. Without my associates, what is the use of the material world? He showed particularly, according to his desire, the destination of those who had gone to his abode.

### Text 21

*arjunaḥ preyasaḥ sakhyuḥ kṛṣṇasya virahāturaḥ*  
*ātmānaṁ sāntvayām āsa kṛṣṇa-gītaiḥ sad-uktibhiḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna felt great distress over separation from Kṛṣṇa, his dear friend. But he consoled himself by remembering the pure words the Lord had sung to him.**

**Комментарий:**

The pure words of Kṛṣṇa were *mām evaiṣyasi satyaṁ te pratijāne priyo’si me*: you will come to me. I promise you since you are dear to me. (BG [7.25](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/7.25)). Mahābhārata says:

*dadarśa tatra govindaṁ brāhmeṇa vapuṣānvitam*  
*tenaiva dṛṣṭa-pūrveṇa sādṛśyenopasūcitam*  
*dīpyamānaṁ sva-vapuṣā divyair astrair upaskṛtam*  
*upāsyamānaṁ vīreṇa phālgunena suvarcasā*  
*yathā svarūpaṁ kaunteyas dadarśa madhusūdanam*  
*tāv ubhau puruṣa-vyāghrau samudvīkṣya yudhiṣṭhiram \|*  
*yathārhaṁ pratipedāte pūjayā deva-pūjitau \|\|*

**Yudhiṣṭhira saw Kṛṣṇa endowed with a spiritual body, which was similar to the form he had previously seen. Kṛṣṇa was shining with his body, decorated with attractive weapons, and was worshipped by brave Arjuna who was also shining. He saw Kṛṣṇa in his svarūpa. Seeing Yudhiṣṭhira, the two heroes, worshipable by the devatās met him and worshipped him.**

### Text 23

*dvārakāṁ hariṇā tyaktāṁ samudro ’plāvayat kṣaṇāt*  
*varjayitvā mahā-rāja śrīmad-bhagavad-ālayam*

**Перевод:**

**As soon as Dvārakā was abandoned by the Supreme Lord, the ocean flooded it on all sides, O King, sparing only his palace.**

**Комментарий:**

**Kṛṣṇa sandarbha 181:**

By material vision, he left Dvārakā. But the next verse says he lives there eternally. Thus he did not give up Dvārakā (*hariṇā atyaktām* instead of *hariṇā tyaktām*). In any case the water spread everywhere like a moat, covering everything. The ocean submerged everything by the order of the Lord. There was an external covering (in the form of flooding) of the houses of material persons, who were sent to Hastināpura. The place was destroyed in this way because, built by Viśvakarmā, it was mixed with the material world for prakaṭa pastimes. And Sudharma hall came from Svarga. In the aprakaṭa pastimes, there is another more splendid hall than that. However the ocean did not destroy the palaces of the Lord, the glorious houses of the Yadus. Even today one can sometimes see something far off in the ocean. The greatness of the place is famous. Addressing Parīkṣit as *mahārāja* (great king) indicates the fame of the place. Or *mahārāja* is a modifier: the ocean flooded everything except the city of Dvārakā, the eternal abode of Kṛṣṇa (*bhagavad-ālayam)* where the great Yadu kings (*mahārāja*) resided. Not only did the city remain, but in the palace Kṛṣṇa eternally resides (*nityam sannihitaḥ*). Why would the inhabitants not live there? The word Bhagavān means the Lord endowed with all his powers such as the Yādavas.

The place is described. By remembering the place, since one cannot see the manifested place, one destroys all contamination. There is no possibility of the place of the Lord being otherwise.

Viṣṇu Purāṇa says:

*plāvayāmāsa tāṁ śūnyāṁ dvārakāṁ ca mahodadhiḥ \|*  
*nityaṁ sannihitas tatra bhagavān keśavo yataḥ \|\|*  
*tad atīva mahā-puṇyaṁ sarva-pāpa-praṇāśanam \|*  
*viṣṇu-krīḍānvitaṁ sthānaṁ dṛṣṭvā pāpāt pramucyate \|\|*

**The ocean flooded the empty city of Dvārakā. Since Kṛṣṇa resides there eternally, seeing that place most pure, filled with the Lord’s pastimes, destroyer of all sins, one becomes free of sin. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa** [**5.38.9-10**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vp/5.38.9)**)**

In Hari-vaṁśa Nārada, sent by Indra, says to the Yadus:

*kṛṣṇo bhogavatīṁ ramyām ṛṣi-kāntāṁ mahā-yaśāḥ \|*  
*dvārakām ātmasāt kṛtvā samudraṁ gamayiṣyati \|\|*

**Kṛṣṇa will make Dvārakā, attractive and full of enjoyment, dear to the sages and most famous, one with himself, and make the ocean flood it. (Hari-vaṁśa 2.102.32)**

*Ātmasāt kṛtvā* means he did not leave the place.

### Text 28

*itthaṁ harer bhagavato rucirāvatāra-*  
*vīryāṇi bāla-caritāni ca śantamāni*  
*anyatra ceha ca śrutāni gṛṇan manuṣyo*  
*bhaktiṁ parāṁ paramahaṁsa-gatau labheta*

**Перевод:**

**The auspicious exploits of the attractive incarnations of Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord, and also the pastimes he performed as a child in Vṛṇdāvana are described in this** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **and in other scriptures. Anyone who clearly chants these descriptions of his pastimes will attain supreme** ***bhakti*** **to Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is the goal of perfect sages.**

**Комментарий:**

The result of hearing about Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes is attainment of Kṛṣṇa and the highest bhakti. Those who hear the pastimes attain supreme bhakti to Kṛṣṇa, the goal of paramahaṁsas who are attracted to his pastimes, what to speak of his devotees.
